# Open questions

Questions for the investigation side. Implementation sessions add here instead of
guessing. Investigation sessions close them by writing or updating a spec.

| ID | Question | Raised by | Status | Answered in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-001 | How is the install laid out: which files hold maps, graphics, configs, scripts and game logic? | setup | answered | `FMT-install-layout.md` |
| Q-002 | What is the map file format (dimensions, terrain, resources, objects, player starts)? | setup | answered (layout); geometry in Q-015 and Q-018 answered; details open in Q-016, Q-017 | `FMT-s2m-map.md`, `MAP-grid-geometry.md` |
| Q-003 | How long is one simulation tick in real time, and is the game logic tick-based at all? (Answered: tick-based, 0.1 s per logic tick, 10 per second. Open: speed control steps, network tick length.) | setup | partly answered | `ECO-time-units.md` |
| Q-004 | How do carriers choose which ware to carry next on a road segment? (Partly answered: idle field, look-for-work timing, choice by transport priority between the two ends, carrying and waiting, ware routes, donkey trigger. Which warehouse sends carriers and donkeys: answered in ECO-REQ.) | setup | partly answered | `TRN-carriers-transport.md`, `ECO-requests-dispatch.md` |
| Q-005 | What are the production times and input/output for each building? (Partly answered: the work cycle of workshop and mine workers and its durations. Woodcutter, stone pit and forester: answered in ECO-OUT; where inputs come from: ECO-REQ-4. Inputs, outputs, stock limits, production and rest times of every building: ECO-CAT; production checks, consumption, product choice (alternation, switch, tools), mine food and ore, input orders, stop rules: ECO-PROD. Mine ore search, farm, fisher, hunter, donkey farm, shipyard boat and ship mode: ECO-FOOD. Ship site: ECO-FOOD-7.3b; launch and ships: SHIP. Open: what a new donkey does first.) | setup | partly answered; fisher, hunter and farmer cycles outlined 2026-10-17 (ECO-OUT-7 .. 10, medium) | `ECO-worker-cycle.md`, `ECO-outdoor-workers.md`, `ECO-buildings-catalog.md`, `ECO-production-chains.md`, `ECO-food-mining.md` |
| Q-006 | How is the common `rc00` file container decoded (compression, cipher, key, meaning of the 8-byte header field)? Blocks Q-002, Q-005 and Q-008. Also referred to as "Q-container". | Q-001 survey | answered | `FMT-rc00-container.md` |
| Q-007 | Which game rules are hard-coded in the executable rather than set from `data/scripts/properties`? (Carrier choice, path finding, combat, territory are presumed code.) | Q-001 survey | answered by the specs as they stand (sweep 2026-11-12 night): the property scripts (`LUA-properties-api.md`) give the tables only: building types (size, costs, workers, products, ranges, remains), goods, jobs and tools, animals, terrain patterns' properties. Everything that decides behaviour is code, read and specified as such: transport and carrier choice (TRN-CARRY, ECO-DIST, ECO-REQ), road and free path finding (TRN-ROAD, TRN-FREE), work cycles and their timings (ECO-WORK, ECO-OUT, ECO-FOOD), combat and conquest (MIL-ATK, MIL-CON), territory (TER), the computer players (AI-*), the random generator (SIM-RNG) and the game start (SIM-START). Was: open | |
| Q-008 | What values do `data/scripts/properties/*.lua` set, and what do `Jobs`, `streets`, `signs` and `data` define? (API answered; the value tables themselves are read from the scripts at runtime. `Jobs` = professions via `gp_*`, `streets` = `sp_*`, `signs` = `si_*`, `data` = loader.) | Q-001 survey | partly answered | `LUA-properties-api.md` |
| Q-009 | What is the save game format, file extension and location? | Q-001 survey | answered 2026-11-12 (sweep): `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-1 (`<Documents>\S2\saves\<name>.sav`, `mp_saves\<name>.mps`, rc00-wrapped) and FMT-SAV-2 .. 4 (the format, read to the last byte on four saves) | `FMT-savegame.md` |
| Q-010 | What is the layout of the 688-byte lighting presets in `data/settings/*.bin` and of the `.pfx` particle files? | Q-001 survey | partly answered 2026-10-24 (the `.bin` layout and which file is used: GFX-VIEW-2.3b; the `.pfx` layout: GFX-OBJ-8.3a); open: Q-350 | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.3b |
| Q-011 | What do the hex ids in `data/scripts/info/b_*` and `i_*` identify, and what do those scripts set? (Partly known: the ids are the 8-hex ids passed to `bp_create` / `gp_create` in `properties/buildings.lua` and `Goods.lua`; how the id is computed and what the info scripts set is still open.) | Q-001 survey | answered 2026-10-24 (investigation, E-sim-007): a building type's or good's help page runs `b_<id>.lua` / `i_<id>.lua` (id in lower-case hex without leading zeros); the scripts set only labels, wrapped texts and atlas pictures through `ui.infoLabel2`, `ui.infoLabel`, `ui.infoText`, `ui.infoPicture`, their texts being the `dlg_*` keys of `info.txt` | `LUA-properties-api.md` LUA-PROP-12 |
| Q-012 | Do the XML files in `data/settings` hold any gameplay values (footprints, door positions) or presentation only? | Q-001 survey | answered 2026-11-12 night by the specs as they stand (sweep): presentation only. `buildings.xml` gives meshes, site models, door / work clips with their sounds and particles and the colour map (GFX-OBJ, GFX-ANIM); `graphics.xml` render settings (GFX-VIEW, GFX-OBJ-6.1, GFX-WATER); `terrain_static_data.xml` patterns and map-object models (GFX-PAT, GFX-OBJ-1; its `blocking` values are not read, GFX-PAT-1); `items.xml`, `animals.xml`, `ships.xml` models and scales; `flocks.xml` the decorative birds, fish and bugs; `daytimes.xml` is not read (the `.bin` table is, GFX-VIEW-2.3b); `decals.xml`, `engine.xml` presentation. Footprints, costs, jobs and every other rule value come from `data/scripts/properties` (LUA-properties-api) and the code; door places are the building models' door bones (GFX-ANIM-6). Was: open | |
| Q-013 | Does a save file (`.sav`) use the rc00 container with the fixed base key, as `FMT-rc00-container.md` FMT-RC-2.3 infers? Needs a real save. Related to Q-009. | Q-006 | answered 2026-11-12 (sweep): yes, the base key unchanged (FMT-RC-2.3, FMT-SAV-1.2); four saves decode so, two of them renamed by hand | `FMT-rc00-container.md` FMT-RC-2.3 |
| Q-014 | What unit are the times in `properties/*.lua` (e.g. production time 333, construct time 55, time classes 50..400)? Related to Q-003. | Q-006 | answered | `ECO-time-units.md` |
| Q-015 | Map grid geometry: which six neighbours does a field have (row offset), what is the world size of a field and the unit of the height values? (Answered: odd rows half a field toward +x, six neighbours, no wrap; field spacing 6.0 world units; 1000 height units = 1 world unit. Rest moved to Q-023.) | Q-002 | answered | `MAP-grid-geometry.md` |
| Q-016 | What do the per-player fields and the three info values in the map header mean (`FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-2.5 to 2.7)? | Q-002 | partly answered 2026-11-12 (sweep): the per-player fields are FMT-S2M-2.5 (kind) and 2.6 (tribe, colour, team, strength; 2026-11-04, Q-1007); of the three closing values the first (0 / 4: the end-of-game flow) and the third (map kind, FMT-SAV-2.3) are named, the second (always 0, read from map version 4, FMT-SAV-4.1a) is not | `FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-2.5 .. 2.7 |
| Q-017 | What are trigger kinds 2 and 3 and how large is a trigger region? | Q-002 | answered by existing rules (sweep 2026-11-12 night): `CAM-campaign-missions.md` CAM-2.1, 2.2: kind 1 = the player has explored the trigger's field, 2 = the player's territory covers it, 3 = the same and reaching it wins the mission; the condition is tested on the trigger's one field, so a region has no size beyond that field (a kind-3 trigger's surroundings within radius 2 start explored, CAM-3.1b). Was: open | |
| Q-018 | Confirm in code which field-state bit is Water and which is Coast (data says bit 2 = sea; editor name order suggests the opposite). (Answered: bit 2 = Water, bit 3 = Coast, from the resource placement and building-site code.) | Q-002 | answered | `MAP-grid-geometry.md` MAP-GRID-7 |
| Q-019 | Measure one production cycle in the running game to confirm that `bp_setProductionTime` is in ticks and what else a cycle includes. (Answered: sawing measured at about 33 s for 333 ticks; the cycle also has taking the product, the walk to the flag and back, and the rest. Follow-up measurements moved to Q-028.) | Q-014 | answered | `ECO-time-units.md`, `ECO-worker-cycle.md` |
| Q-020 | What script API do map scripts and the menu configuration use (`menu.*`, `pl_*`, `map_*`, callbacks `onLoaded`, `onStarted`, `onLogicTick`, `onTriggerIsActive`)? | Q-008 | answered 2026-11-13 (sweep, by rules written since): map scripts use only the `menu` table and `triggers.removeTrigger` (every call in the shipped campaign and tutorial scripts: `CAM-campaign-missions.md` CAM-1.3, 1.4) and the callbacks `onLoaded`, `onStarted`, `onLogicTick(seconds)`, `onTriggerIsActive(name)` (CAM-1.2); `menu.cfg` calls `setLanguage`, `setPlayerColor`, `setVideoVoiceStream` (CAM-1.3, `GFX-objects.md` player colours); the properties scripts' API is `LUA-properties-api.md`. No shipped script calls a `pl_*` or `map_*` function. Was: open | |
| Q-021 | FMT-RC-2.2 says "convert to lower case" and FMT-RC-2.4 hashes "the bytes" of the name. For a base name with non-ASCII characters: which characters are lower-cased (ASCII only, or by code page/locale) and in which byte encoding is the name hashed? No shipped file needs this. Until answered, `assets::rc00` refuses to derive a name-dependent key from a non-ASCII base name (`.s2m`/`.sav` are unaffected). | FMT-RC implementation | partly answered 2026-11-12 (sweep, E-sav-016, code reading): the name is lower-cased byte by byte with the C runtime's locale-dependent `tolower` over the bytes the program holds (the ANSI code page bytes of the path), and the CRC-32 runs over those bytes; in the runtime's default "C" locale only A .. Z change. The runtime's locale functions are linked in; whether the game ever switches the locale is not traced | |
| Q-022 | What does the game do with a file that starts with the rc00 magic but is shorter than the 20-byte header (FMT-RC-1.1)? `assets::rc00` treats it as a fatal load error rather than as a plain file (FMT-RC-1.2). | FMT-RC implementation | open | |
| Q-023 | Screen orientation of the grid: which way +x and +y point on screen and on the minimap (is row 0 the top or the bottom edge), and what world origin is added to field positions (`MAP-grid-geometry.md` MAP-GRID-5.2)? Presentation only. (Partly answered: by the scripts' direction names +y is north, row 0 the southern edge. Open: confirmation on screen, minimap, world origin.) | Q-015 | partly answered | `MAP-grid-geometry.md` MAP-GRID-1.6 |

Includes the harbor and mine cases and the flag case. (Answered: site rules per size, flag position, slope limits, footprints, state bits and their updates. Open: territory, levelling of construction sites, confirmation in game in Q-031.) | Q-015 | answered | `ECO-building-placement.md` |
| Q-025 | `FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-1.6 lists marker version 0 for `LogicSystem` and for `resources`, but a reader written from the spec finds version 7 on `LogicSystem` and version 4 on `resources` in all 34 release maps (every other listed version matches). Which is right, and are FMT-S2M-3.1 and 5.1 the layouts of versions 7 and 4? With these two values accepted the rest of the layout parses to the last byte. (Answered: the implementation is right. Both are counted in all 34 maps and are the versions the game writes; FMT-S2M-3 and 5 are the layouts of versions 7 and 4. Corrected, with the loader's version tests.) | FMT-S2M implementation | answered | `FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-1.6, 3.1a, 5.1 |
| Q-026 | FMT-S2M-1.3 says a field cursor addresses a field (`0 <= x < width`) and FMT-S2M-5.4 says an animal's from / to / next cursors equal the current field when idle. In the 34 release maps the third `Movement Base` cursor ("next") is `(0xFFFFFFFF, 0xFFFFFFFF)` for 3927 of 4284 animals; no other field cursor in any map is out of range. Is all-bits-set a "none" cursor, and where else may it occur? (Answered: yes, all bits set is the none cursor, the value before first use. The third cursor is the target of the current or last step, none until the animal first steps. In maps it occurs nowhere else; in saves expect it in any unused movement cursor.) | FMT-S2M implementation | answered | `FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-1.3, 5.4 |
| Q-027 | Many objects in `FMT-s2m-map.md` carry a `bool` that the spec shows only as 1 (after the marker of `Core UUID`, `TriggerSystem`, `TriggerObject`, `MapSystem`, every map layer, `resources`, `Resources AnimalRespawn`, `DoodadsSystem`, `Logic Ambients`, and inside `LogicSystem`). What does it mean and what follows when it is 0? `assets::s2m` rejects 0 there. Same for the `bool` (0) inside a trigger object, which is stored as read. | FMT-S2M implementation | partly answered 2026-11-12 (sweep, E-sav-016, code reading): the loaders of `Core UUID`, `MapSystem`, `ElevationMap`, `PatternMap`, `GridStatesMap`, `TriggerSystem`, `TriggerObject`, `Resources AnimalRespawn` and `Map Exploration` read the `bool` into the object and go on with the same fields whatever its value (no branch on it), so a reader may accept 0, keep it and read on; what the flag means per class (valid / in use) is not traced | |
| Q-028 | In-game measurements to settle walking speed and the worker cycle (to be made by Jens, normal speed). (a) A carrier walking flag to flag on a straight road of 6 or more fields on the flattest ground available, each direction timed separately: `TRN-settler-movement.md` predicts 2.22 s per field on level ground (6 fields: 13.4 s); about 2.78 s per field in both directions would mean a rule is missing. (b) The same on a clearly sloping road, uphill and downhill timed separately: predicted slower uphill only. (c) Sawmill with logs in stock: saw stops until the saw starts again (predicted 12.3 s) and plank laid at the flag until the next plank laid at the flag (predicted 45.6 s), and which moment "end of one plank" was in the 8 to 9 s measurement (`ECO-worker-cycle.md` T2, T3, T6). | Q-019 | open | |
| Q-029 | LUA-PROP-2.1 says the `*_create` argument is a string of 1 to 8 hex digits, but one call in the shipped `sounds.lua` passes `"0xcea2bcc3"` (with a `0x` prefix; a sound of type effect, not referenced by any map). How does the engine read it: as `0xCEA2BCC3`, as something else, or does the create fail? Every other id in the scripts has the specified form. (Answered: the id string is read up to the first non-hex character, so `"0xcea2bcc3"` gives id 0; the create succeeds.) | LUA-PROP implementation | answered | `LUA-properties-api.md` LUA-PROP-2.1a |
| Q-030 | `LUA-properties-api.md` says the engine registers 141 functions and the shipped scripts call 129, but the spec's own tables list 148 functions, and the scripts call 136 of them (148 minus the 12 marked "not called", which is exactly the set the scripts never call). Which numbers are right, and if 141, which 7 rows of the tables are not registered? (Answered: 148 names are registered (147 handlers; `bp_setSecondGoods` and `bp_setAlternativeGoods` share one), the scripts call 136, the 12 marked "not called" are right. 141 / 129 were miscounts and are corrected.) | LUA-PROP implementation | answered | `LUA-properties-api.md` |

Start a free game on `MP_4P_Fight` as the player whose headquarters stands at field (27,57) (the south-west start; see `MAP-grid-geometry.md` MAP-GRID-1.6 for orientation). Open the build menu on the fields around the headquarters and compare the offered sizes with `ECO-building-placement.md` T12 (only fields inside your territory count). Also: is the headquarters' flag on its south-east side; can a building be placed directly next to the headquarters (expected: no); does a large building become impossible two fields from it (expected: yes, medium is offered there). | ECO-PLACE | open | |
| Q-032 | Roads and flags, not traced in the binary (Jens, original game, and investigation): (a) when a road is finished, does the game set flags along it by itself? (b) when a road is removed, what happens to the carrier and the wares on it? (c) when a flag is removed, are its roads and the building behind it removed too? (d) when two routes have the same cost, which is taken (`TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-2.4)? (e) check T3 of `TRN-roads-flags.md`: on `tutorial01`, build a road from the headquarters flag two rows north and see whether it goes over the north-east neighbour of the flag. (Partly answered: a carrier whose road is removed walks to a warehouse, or dies if it cannot reach one, `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-7. Removal answered by Q-139, `TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-6. Still open: automatic flags, route ties.) | TRN-ROAD | answered 2026-11-16 night by existing code reading (E-trn-006 / E-ui-006 road creation; TRN-ROAD-2.4): (a) **no automatic flags**: building a road places flags only at its two ends (a flag is added at the end only when the end lies on another road where a flag may stand, splitting it); the fields between get the road and nothing else, so a long road keeps one carrier until the player sets flags; (d) equal-cost routes are decided by TRN-ROAD-2.4's read-out alone (keep the previous direction, else the lowest direction number), T18 to T20; (b), (c) by TRN-CARRY-7 / TRN-ROAD-6; (e) is TRN-ROAD T3 | `TRN-carriers-transport.md` |
| Q-033 | What type does a good have when the scripts never call `gp_setType` for it (LUA-PROP-5)? In the shipped scripts 31 of the 67 goods (all of them wares by name: wood, stone, fish, ...) have no `gp_setType` call; 30 are set to settler and 6 to ware group, and none is set to ware (0) explicitly. Is the engine default "ware"? `s2-rules` leaves the type unset. (Answered: yes, a new good has type 0, ware.) | LUA-PROP implementation | answered | `LUA-properties-api.md` LUA-PROP-5 |
| Q-034 | Territory and exploration at the start of a game. ECO-PLACE and TRN-ROAD test cases assume player 0 owns and has explored every field; the simulation does the same until territory is specified, so ECO-PLACE-3.1 / 2.1 and TRN-ROAD-1.3 are trivially true for that player. With this assumption and no Border bits set (maps carry none, and ECO-PLACE-1.6 does no recalculation on load), the class counts of ECO-PLACE T13 match exactly. Which fields does a player own and see at the start, and is Border then computed? (Partly answered: at game start the player owns every field within distance 9 of the headquarters and has explored every field within 12, except the outermost map rows and columns. Open: owner rule where claims overlap.) (Overlap rule: ECO-HQ-3.5.) | ECO-PLACE implementation | answered | `ECO-warehouse-construction.md` ECO-HQ-3 |
| Q-035 | May a second road join the same two flags along another route (TRN-ROAD)? The route rules only forbid crossing and passing flags, so the simulation allows it. | TRN-ROAD implementation | answered 2026-11-12 night (E-trn-008, code reading; `TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-2.6): yes. Making a road checks only the flags at its two ends (present, different), not whether the two flags are already joined; walks choose among parallel roads by TRN-FREE-6.5 (f). Was: open | |
| Q-036 | ECO-PLACE-4.2 says the classes "around a change" are recomputed but not how far. Does the game recompute a smaller area, so that its cached classes can differ from the rules? | ECO-PLACE implementation | open | |

Also: (e) put a coin and a plank at the two ends at once and see which goes first (coin expected), (f) how long a busy road takes to get a donkey. | TRN-CARRY | open | |
| Q-038 | Not traced in ECO-HQ: how a warehouse decides which settler, carrier or donkey to send to which road or building and in which order; how a ware's destination building is chosen; when a finished building requests its worker and its inputs; the owner of a field claimed by several military buildings; how leveller and builder are chosen. | Q-039 | How does a settler walk over open terrain (not along roads): woodcutter, stonemason and forester to their target and back, settlers leaving a warehouse for their job? (Answered: backward search from the target with step cost 3 / uphill 4, 5, 7 / +2 per tree field, stop when the walker is reached, route by smallest labels with direction kept or random ties; stop short 1 field then a straight fine approach; road walk for settlers sent to buildings.) | ECO-OUT, ECO-REQ | answered | `TRN-free-walk.md` |

| Q-049 | What are the building `_L` textures and the `DiffuseColor0` vertex colour of building and object meshes used for (GFX-GR2-5.4)? | GFX-GR2 | answered 2026-11-08 b by existing rules: `_L` = the house light map (GFX-OBJ-2.1, Self-Illumination binding, E-gfx-021); the vertex colour is unused by houses (2.1) and multiplies the light on trees (GFX-OBJ tree shader); GFX-GR2-5.4 updated. | Q-051 | How does a free game set each player's tribe (0 Romans, 1 Africans, 2 Chinese): chosen in the setup screen per slot, taken from the map's player record (FMT-S2M-2.6 field 4), or fixed? What is the default, and do computer players get one at random? Needed for the builder's construction positions (TRN-FREE-5.5b) and tribe-specific graphics. (Answered: chosen per slot in the setup screen with a tribe icon picker and copied unchanged into the player; the map's player record is not used; host slot starts with tribe 0, setup defaults and non-lobby starts are tribe 0. Computer default moved to Q-053.) | Q-120 (investigation) | answered | `UI-game-setup.md` |
| Q-052 | Does placing a well (spring) check for ground water? The help text says wells must be built over sweet water, but `water` has no per-field resource flag in the shipped `Goods.lua` (commented out) and the production step checks nothing (ECO-PROD-2.5). Trace the building-site check for the spring and the geologist's water sign. (Answered: no. Placing a well uses only the small-building rules and production checks nothing. The geologist reports water, amount 1, on every ForBuilding field (field state bit 9), whatever the resource layer holds; water resources can only lie on ForBuilding fields and are unused.) | ECO-PROD (investigation) | answered | `ECO-production-chains.md` ECO-PROD-2.5a..c |
| Q-053 | Which tribe does a computer slot get when it is added in the setup screen (fixed, copied, or random), and what happens when a slot's tribe is still unset (-1) at game start (UI-SETUP-1.2, 2.1)? Also: which start modes use the non-lobby path that gives every player tribe 0 (UI-SETUP-2.4). Trace the setup-screen slot widgets or check in the game. (Answered: a new computer slot is computer, strength 0, tribe 0, position/colour/team unset; single-player setup makes slots 1..5 computers that way; when the host switches a slot to computer, the row's tribe is written over it. The start checks only readiness and distinct start positions; tribe -1 would be copied unchanged; implementations treat it as 0. Which modes use the non-lobby start: moved to Q-054.) | Q-051 (investigation) | answered | `UI-game-setup.md` UI-SETUP-1.6, 1.7, 2.6 |
| Q-054 | Which start modes use the non-lobby game setup that gives all 8 players tribe 0 (UI-SETUP-2.4: campaign, tutorial, loaded save?), and do campaign scripts change a player's tribe afterwards? (Answered: campaign and add-on campaign use it; tutorials set only kinds and keep the reset tribe 0; loaded saves refill the slots from the saved setup; free games use the setup screen; scripts cannot set a tribe, only `pl_getTribe` exists.) | Q-053 (investigation) | answered | `UI-game-setup.md` UI-SETUP-2.4 |
| Q-055 | Water rendering leftovers (GFX-WATER-2.5, 4.4, 6.1): reference height of the world-map depth term that fades shore water, which call draws the caustics pass and the mirror image, source and unit of the shader render clock, corner layout of the map-edge skirt. (Answered: the depth term's level and reference are both 0 (never set), so it is a step, 1 below height 0 and 0 above; the caustics pass is a second terrain draw with RenderUnderwaterSurface when shader quality >= 2 and the frame's draw flag asks for it; mirror technique chosen by a caller flag. Open: where the mirror image is rendered, the render clock source and unit, the skirt corners.) | water rendering (investigation) | answered 2026-11-11 (E-gfx-051): the skirt corners were in GFX-WATER-6.1 since 2026-10-17; read again, every piece is now given as a box in world terms (the column pieces reach 3 and 4 units of 3.0 into the map, not along the edge). Before: partly answered; 2026-11-08 b (E-gfx-041; GFX-WATER-2.4): the render clock is the game time in seconds (frozen while paused, faster at higher speeds); the mirror image is drawn by the mirror pass of GFX-WATER-2.6a (answered 2026-11-06); open: the skirt corners only. Was: partly answered. **Correction 2026-11-09 (Q-1018, E-gfx-045): the two levels are not 0: a static initializer sets level -100 and reference -3000, so the term is a ramp from -100 down to -3000 and the surface lies at -0.1 (GFX-WATER-3.3, 4.4)** | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-2.5, 2.6, 4.4 |
| Q-056 | Coins and promotion leftovers (MIL-PROM-3, 4): default of a military building's coin supply switch; the per-building number `k` that phases the 33-tick coin check; the per-building divisor `n` of the coin order choice; the promotion counter's value at game start. (Answered: switch on at creation; k = low 32 bits of the building's object number; n = enemy distance (nearest occupied enemy military building, 1000 if none; -1 before the first update); promotion counter starts at 300, first run tick 300.) | soldier promotion (investigation) | answered | `MIL-coins-promotion.md` MIL-PROM-3.1 to 3.3, 4.1 |
| Q-057 | Distribution leftovers (ECO-DIST-1.7, 3.3): the per-good condition under which a good with a distribution table is still served by the plain longest-waiting rule; where a settler taken out of a warehouse goes; the initial stamp of a new order. (Answered: orders of finished warehouse-type buildings form list 0, which always uses the longest-waiting rule; a taken-out settler is made without job and goes home to another accepting warehouse (medium); new order stamp 0, strict lowest, list order on ties.) | plan 3.4 (investigation) | answered | `ECO-distribution.md` ECO-DIST-1.7a, 3.3a, 3.3b |
| Q-058 | Animal roaming (ECO-FOOD-5.7): an animal's wait time, how it picks a walk target within `an_setRange` of its home and `an_setMoveDistance`, the per-kind limit of two walk starts per tick, `an_setAvoidBuildings`, and walk speed. Matters for hunters (where the animal is when the search runs). (Answered: wait W+1 updates; a global ticket scheme allows two counted starts per tick; target = N-th acceptable field (N random 1..3md(md+1)) in a breadth-first search from home (or from the current field when farther than range), within range of the current field, not road/mining/border/harbour, no animal, no building within 2; failure -> ticket 100; after a walk wait = trunc(seconds x idle factor 3.0 x 10).) | Q-132 (investigation) | answered | `ECO-food-mining.md` ECO-FOOD-5.7 to 5.11 |
| Q-059 | Border markers (TER-7.1): which points get a `limes` stone or a `buoy` (every Border field, or between neighbouring Border fields of one owner), how they are oriented and spaced, and whether unexplored or other players' borders are drawn. Presentation only. (Answered: one marker on every Border field of each player, at the field's own position, buoy on Water fields, stone elsewhere; no rotation or spacing rule; all players' markers built; buoys bob 0.05 x sin; rebuilt on territory change.) | plan 4.1 (investigation) | answered | `TER-territory.md` TER-7.2 to 7.4 |
| Q-060 | Soldier leftovers (MIL-SOL-1.2, 1.3, 7.1): a soldier's health after a promotion (kept, or set to the new rank's maximum); which soldier strikes first in a duel and how the rounds are counted (plan 4.4); the slider range of the military settings dialog; whether computer players change the recruit count of 10. (Answered: health kept; first striker = coin flip drawn when the attacker is sent, then alternating, rounds every 17 ticks, hit applied at the next round's start; sliders 0..1 (step count still open, Q-061); computer players never change the recruit count.) | plan 4.3 (investigation) | answered | `MIL-attack-combat.md` MIL-ATK-6, 7.1, 7.3, 7.4; `MIL-soldiers.md` |
| Q-061 | Attack leftovers (MIL-ATK-1.4, 5.3, 6.6, 7.3): which attack-dialog count button has which fraction (read from label order); the number of steps of the settings sliders; the exact walk target of a target's defender and what each kind of surviving soldier does after a duel in every case. (Answered: Low 0.25, Medium 0.5, High 0.75, Highest 1.0; military sliders continuous (no step count); target defender walks to its paired attacker's waiting field; survivors: attacker back to waiting, interceptor waits until re-paired or its building has no attackers left, target defender walks in and comes out again if re-paired.) | plan 4.5 (investigation) | answered | `MIL-attack-combat.md` MIL-ATK-1.4, 3.4, 5.3, 6.6, 7.3 |
| Q-062 | Conquest leftovers (MIL-CON-1.2, 4.1): what the ruin of a burnt military building or warehouse holds; in which order several waiting attackers test the taking condition in one tick; what one building-window command does (sent next to the attack and catapult dialogs; its number in the original's program is kept with E-mil-005). (Answered: remains = halved-up share of the used frame and main material plus unused site material, put on the building's flag up to the flag limit, rest lost; the ruin holds nothing; each waiting attacker tests in its own update and all that pass walk to the door, the first to arrive takes it; that command is the harbour's `prepare-expedition`.) | plan 4.5 (investigation) | answered | `MIL-conquest.md` MIL-CON-1.2, 4.4; `SHIP-ships-expeditions.md` |
| Q-063 | SHIP-4.1: how a harbour's list of needed goods is filled: which requests on an island that lacks the good become harbour needs, when, and in which amounts. | plan 4.7 (investigation) | partly answered 2026-11-16 night (E-ship-006, code reading): a ware order that finds no warehouse with the good over land, and a settler request that finds no warehouse able to send one, is attached to the player's finished harbour that is road-reachable from the requester's flag, accepts the good and has a docking place on a sea with a finished ship of the player; lowest road cost wins, first in the harbour list on ties. The need is the number of attached requesters. Open: when a waiting request is retried, and whether a ware order is detached when its good turns up on land (SHIP-4.1, T19); 2026-11-16 day (E-ship-007, code reading): the attach is a step of the 11-tick ware order pass (a settler request: its turn in the settler queue), so an unattached order is retried at the next pass; an attached ware order is re-checked at every pass that finds no warehouse over land and detached when its harbour no longer accepts the good or is no longer road-reachable; a ware order served over land is not detached in that branch (its link ends with the order, medium). Left: the order's end and the need count until then | |
| Q-064 | SHIP-3.1: the ship route search over water: step cost, tie-breaking, and what happens when another ship blocks the route or the target. | plan 4.7 (investigation) | partly answered 2026-11-02: the free walk's search and drawing read-out, with a field test (Water, not DoodadBlocked) and a step cost of 1, or 3 beside land, landing fields or the map's edge. (Answered: construction, needs, site search, roads, demolition, production control, tools, food in `AI-construction.md`; military buildings, upgrades, attacks, soldiers, weapons, coins, catapults, expansion targets in `AI-military.md`; ships, expeditions, activation, profiles in `AI-misc.md`. | Q-068 | AI-2.6: the destruction system's test for a building that is no longer useful, and the four flags that protect a building from it. (Answered: only resource buildings and hunters are tested, protected are military, depot, planting (forester, farm) and harbour buildings; a building is torn down when its site value is 0, or it is a fisher with productivity 0, unless it is the only woodcutter of its cell; plus cell clearing every 59 ticks and dead flags every tick.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-9 |
| Q-069 | AI-2.1: when a computer player is re-initialised instead of thinking, and what it keeps. (Answered: when it has no cells left (all land lost); it deletes every record, resets every part, clears the profile and switches itself off; nothing is kept and it is not switched on again. Activation happens at game creation with the profile from the setup strength.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-misc.md` AI-MISC-2 |
| Q-070 | AI-1.2, 1.3: which AI parts read which profile value, and how `bp_setAiScore`, `bp_setAiThreat` and `gp_setAiPriority` are used. (Partly answered: aiThreat and aiScore feed the military map (AI-MIL-1.1) and the attack and site scores; aiPriority weights the needs construction score (AI-CON-4.2); profile values: AI-CON-2 sawmills/smithies/mints, AI-MIL-2 strength/intercepting/mode, AI-MIL-5.4 mode, AI-MIL-6 smithies/mints/catapults/coin arrangement, AI-CON-8 optimizers. Summary in `AI-misc.md` AI-MISC-3.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-misc.md` AI-MISC-3 |
| Q-071 | AI-2.7: the per-tick update of the AI's task objects: caller, order relative to the think, and what building, need and production tasks do each tick. (Answered: the update only re-resolves references between buildings, needs, productions, cells and game objects and refreshes the buildings' protection flags; no decisions. Caller order still untraced.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-1.3 |

(Answered: the road requests a helper in the first tick it has both flags and no carrier; the warehouse with the lowest route cost that has a helper, or can make one, sends it from the next tick on, at most one settler per 30 ticks per warehouse; at most 4 requests are tried per tick. The walk to the road is Q-039.) | TRN-CARRY implementation | answered | `ECO-requests-dispatch.md` ECO-REQ-1.1, ECO-REQ-2, ECO-REQ-3 |

(Answered: the number of the player's ware objects of the good = wares outside buildings, lying at a flag or carried; stock itself is the sum of all buildings' stock entries; both refreshed on a 22-tick cycle.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-1.7 |

(Answered: the number of fields the player owns (regions flagged as not counting, probably water, excluded), computed by the statistics once per 601 ticks.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-1.8 |

(Answered: per good the first type in type-id order whose up-to-four product slots include it; mine table = types of size mine plus the fisher, normal table = all others; flesh is hard-wired to the slaughterhouse, no mine entry; stone: pit / stone mine.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-1.4 |

(Answered: it sets warehouse stop/out flags: step 0 plank/stone/coin when the spread between storehouses exceeds 40 (stop everywhere, out at the fullest), step 1 clears; steps 2/7 soldiers: out on in warehouses whose cell has no military building with enemy distance < 36, steps 3/8 clear; other steps idle.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-10.4 |

Intent unknown; specified as read.) | plan 5.1 (investigation) | answered | `AI-military.md` AI-MIL-4.3 |

| Q-103 | Wares are given a destination flag until building destinations are specified (TRN-CARRY-5.6). When one is put down at its destination flag the simulation counts it delivered and removes it (standing in for TRN-CARRY-3.3's hand-over to a building). | Q-104 | TRN-CARRY T1 has the carrier start picking up in the tick it reaches A (23 -> 27 -> 31) but start putting down one tick after it reaches B (76 -> 81 -> 85, back at M 108). The rules as written give 76 -> 80 -> 84 -> 107, which is within the test's stated tolerance. Is there a one-tick step on arrival with a ware (for example the room check at the flag) that the rules do not mention? Measured 2026-10-24 (E-trace-005, s2-reborn's first difference at trace tick 633): carrier 90234 reaching the flag (139, 48) with a ware for the next road stands in 633 (state 31, Stand), puts down 634 .. 637 (PutDown), picks up 638 .. 641 (Pickup), stands in 642 (state 28) and has its walk at progress 0 in 643 (state 29); s2-reborn starts the put-down in the arrival tick (633) and walks in 641. So there seem to be a standing tick on arrival with a ware and one after the pick-up action before the walk; please specify them (TRN-CARRY-3.3, 3.5) | TRN-CARRY implementation | answered 2026-10-24 (investigation, TRN-CARRY-3.3a, T1): the tick a carrying walk ends on the other flag is a standing tick, the put-down's two actions follow (A + 1 .. A + 8), and without an exchange ware a second standing tick (A + 9) comes before the walk to the idle field or a second pick-up (A + 10); with an exchange the walk back starts in A + 9. At the fetch flag the pick-up starts in the arrival tick. A warehouse helper's put-down also starts in the arrival tick (ECO-WORK-3.8d corrected: helpers do not stand). Measured over all carrier stops of E-trace-005/006 (two fetch-walk ends of 280 stand first, unexplained) | `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-3.3, 3.5 |
| Q-105 | The deadlock swap of TRN-CARRY-3.2 (every 200 ticks of waiting, exchange wares with "the carrier of the neighbouring road that waits towards it") is not implemented: which carriers qualify, where they stand, what is exchanged, and where each then goes are not specified. (Answered: every 200 ticks of waiting, if R2's carrier is idle on its idle field or is waiting with a ware bound over this road, the two carriers exchange roads; wares stay with their carriers.) | TRN-CARRY implementation | answered | `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-3.2, 3.6 |
| Q-106 | During a pickup (drop time then pickup time, TRN-CARRY-3.1), when does the ware stop counting as lying at the flag (for room, TRN-CARRY-4, and route costs, TRN-CARRY-5.3): when the carrier arrives, between the two actions, or at the end? The simulation removes it at the end. (Answered: between the two actions, in the tick the drop-time action ends.) | TRN-CARRY implementation | answered | `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-3.1 |
| Q-107 | A carrier walking back to its idle field looks for work every tick (TRN-CARRY-2.2). If it finds some in the middle of a step, does it turn back at once or finish the step first? | Q-110 | ECO-HQ-4.7 says the building is complete when the main part's remaining work "falls below 0", while T8 gives exactly 2 x 220 + 2 x 220 = 880 ticks. The simulation counts whole ticks of work and finishes a part when n x 220 ticks are done (no extra tick), takes a unit's material when the unit starts, and has the builder start working in the tick it is put on a levelled site. Is there a tick more at the end of a part or the building, and is the material taken at the start of the unit's first work step? (Answered: material taken at unit start; 55 decrements per step then 1 tick; one extra decrement at unit end, so 221 per unit; sawmill 882 work ticks; completion in the tick the main part goes below 0.) | ECO-HQ implementation | answered | `ECO-warehouse-construction.md` ECO-HQ-4.6, 4.6a, 4.7 |
| Q-111 | Which route does a settler take over the roads from its warehouse to its road, site or building, and home again (TRN-CARRY-1.4, ECO-REQ-3.6, TRN-CARRY-7.1)? (Answered: road walk uses plain road length L, no penalties; going home: lowest road route cost from the flag at the settler's field among accepting warehouses, then from the field SE of it, then nearest warehouse on the same landmass via the nearest flag within 30 fields; 51 retries.) | ECO-REQ implementation | answered | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.1, 6.4 |

(Answered: walk in one field, hand over in the next tick without a timed action, walk out; no room check when the ware's destination is the building at the next flag.) | ECO-REQ implementation | answered | `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-3.3 |

Which tribe does a player have in a free game, and how is an offset turned into a lattice position? (Partly answered: a position is the sample of the building's **flag field** plus the offset in sample steps (x columns, y rows), truncated to whole numbers, no odd-row correction; positions visited 0..3. The tribe is the owner's, a per-player game-setup value (with team, colour, HQ), chosen per slot in the setup screen: `UI-game-setup.md`.) | TRN-FREE implementation | partly answered | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-5.5a..c, T15 |

Is that the case meant? (Answered: yes, that reading is right. The other carrier's ware is being carried to F, it waits for room there, and its next flag after F is the far end of this carrier's road.) | TRN-CARRY implementation | answered | `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-3.6 (b), T13 |
| Q-122 | GFX-GR2-2 (Oodle1) is not enough to decode bit for bit. An implementation from the spec, with the open details settled by trying them against the data, expands 195 of 394 sampled sections exactly (every 8th file; exact size, stream ending 1 to 3 bytes short) and diverges on the rest, so no file reads completely yet. Settled by the data, not by the spec: (a) the four size capacities apply to size models 48..63 and 64 (first byte), 32..47 (second), 16..31 (third), 0..15 (fourth); (b) the "max" of the thresholds in 2.4 is the model's largest value, and "at capacity" means capacity + 1 entries with the escape; (c) on the escape with symbols outside the table, a uniform value below 2 equal to 1 picks one of them (uniform), else a new symbol is added; (d) recomputing the table: scale = 8 x 0x4000 / total, each start = previous start + weight x scale / 8; then if the increment is above half its cap the threshold becomes total + cap, else the increment doubles and the threshold becomes total + increment; (e) on reaching the range threshold the weights are halved if the range threshold has reached the weight threshold, else only the table is recomputed; (f) halving truncates, the first heaviest symbol (index 1 up) is swapped with the last, and an escape weight of 0 becomes 1 below capacity; (g) the mid-bits models' capacity is their value range (bits 9..18 of `b` are 0 in every block). Some of these readings may come from familiarity with open-source decoders of this format and need confirming against the binary. Where it still diverges: in section 0 of most files at or just after the first part boundary (first-16-bit offset), the first copy of the new part reaching back past the start of the output; in the large data sections at the first high-bits value 1 (output past 1024 bytes), after the high model gathered several escape-added symbols that can only be 0. Needed: the exact model rules (cumulative table, thresholds, halving, escape and new symbols, including a new symbol whose only possible value already exists), what carries over or restarts at a part boundary (models, previous size symbol, the `produced` count, literal position), the low and mid model capacities, and as test vectors the SHA-256 of the expanded sections of `buildings/PB_R_Sawmill01.GR2`. (Answered: GFX-GR2-2 rewritten as a complete step-by-step algorithm; a second decoder written only from the new text reproduces all 3134 non-empty Oodle1 sections byte for byte. Readings (a) to (g) are right. Likely causes of the divergences: part `i` always uses parameter block `i` (section 0 has parts 0 and 2, part 1 empty), the escape gets +1 before its branch (also when its weight is 0), the high and mid bounds use `produced` of the current part while their thresholds use `R`, the low bound is fixed at min(R + 1, 4), and the table boundary after the last entry is forced to 0x4000. At a part start, models, previous size symbol and `produced` restart; decoder state and output position carry on. Test vectors T9 to T12 (SHA-256 incl. all sawmill sections, first 20 tokens, a table recompute and a halving). No section has three non-empty parts.) | GFX-GR2 implementation | answered | `GFX-granny-format.md` GFX-GR2-2, T9..T12 |
| Q-123 | GFX-GR2-1.6 says the marshalling tables are empty in all files, but some sections list one entry (for example section 3 of `buildings/PB_R_Sawmill01.GR2`). What is that entry, and does a 32-bit little-endian reader need it? (Answered: 16-byte entries {count, offset, type section, type offset}; in 262 files one entry for the skinned vertex array at section 3 offset 0, so a byte-order converter knows how to swap mixed float/byte elements. Not needed by a 32-bit little-endian reader.) | GFX-GR2 implementation | answered | `GFX-granny-format.md` GFX-GR2-1.6 |
| Q-124 | Texture coordinates: the model preview flips V (Max's bottom-left origin to the top-left origin of DDS). Does the game flip V when it builds its vertex buffers, and is that part of the basis conversion of Q-047? (Answered: no. The game copies texture coordinates into its vertex buffers unchanged and the shaders use them unchanged (only a D3D9 half-texel offset); v = 0 is the first stored DDS row. With wgpu (same origin) and DDS rows uploaded in file order, do not flip V. Not part of Q-047.) | GFX-GR2 implementation | answered | `GFX-granny-format.md` GFX-GR2-7.3, 7.4 |
| Q-125 | The builder's timing between construction positions (TRN-FREE-5.5, ECO-HQ-4.6a) is implemented with three assumptions the specs do not state: (a) before its first walk the builder stands on its site's flag field's sample, and after a unit on position 3, so each unit's first walk goes from position 3 to position 0; (b) a walk of n ticks (TRN-FREE-5.3, no carry-over from the previous walk) lets the step's action start n ticks after the walk was ordered, in the 56th tick of the step before, and a walk of 0 ticks starts it in that same tick; (c) a new unit starts in the tick after the previous one ended. Are these right? With them the first plank at the sawmill's flag on tutorial01 falls in tick 3036 (2676 without walking). (Answered: (a) yes for later units; before the first unit the builder also does an approach: from the flag field a fine walk 3 lattice steps north-east and back to the flag field's sample, so unit 1 starts when that ends; (b) yes for n >= 1, but a walk of 0 ticks starts the action in the next tick, not the same; (c) yes: material taken in the tick after the unit ended, and the walk to position 0 is ordered one tick later still. Without material the approach repeats.) | TRN-FREE implementation | answered | `ECO-warehouse-construction.md` ECO-HQ-4.6b, `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-5.5c |
| Q-126 | Terrain textures in the map view: (a) the viewer paints each terrain type from `data/map/textures/<pa_setGfxName>.dds` (LUA-PROP-7), but 20 of the gfx names the scripts give (for example `z_rock_01`, `z_rock_02`, `z_rock_07`, `z_rock_08`, `z_rock_09`, `z_sand_02`, `m_rock_02` to `m_rock_06`, `l_rock_00`, `l_rock_02`, `l_rock_03`, `m_seaground_00`, `m_seaground_01`, `z_border_00`) have no file in `data/map/textures` (some are used on `tutorial01`). Are they unused leftovers, found elsewhere (another folder, another archive), or replaced by another texture? (b) How many fields does one texture repeat over, and is it mapped per field or per triangle? The viewer repeats every 4 fields along a row, a presentation choice. (Answered: the gfx name is looked up in `data/settings/terrain_static_data.xml`, whose pattern entries give the texture file (often not `<name>.dds`) or an alias to another pattern's texture, keeping their own texScale; all 73 names resolve. Texture coordinates = world position x texScale, per vertex and continuous: one repeat = 1 / (6 x texScale) fields along a row, e.g. meadow 2.56 fields.) | map view implementation | answered | `GFX-terrain-patterns.md` |
| Q-127 | How does a mine find its ore field (ECO-PROD-3.3 says only that a current field exists or not)? The simulation takes the first field with the mine's product as resource within the working radius (3) in ring order (centre, then rings 1 to 3, MAP-GRID-3.1), keeps it until it is empty, and does not begin a cycle while there is none. Which fields qualify (radius, ring order, only own territory, ground type), when is the search made, how is a field released when its mine is removed, and is there a "No resources found." message for mines? (Answered: a flood from the mine's field within the working radius 3, first field in flood order holding the product's resource; tried every 101 ticks while the worker is there and the mine has no current field; nothing is reserved; message "No resources found." when switched on and productivity below 0.5. Implemented.) | ECO-PROD implementation | answered | `ECO-food-mining.md` ECO-FOOD-2 |
| Q-128 | The cycles of farm, fisher and hunter are stand-ins (Q-005): the worker produces its product inside the building after the production time (farm 83, fisher 333, hunter 55) and consumes nothing, without a field, fish resource, game animal or planting, and the production switch has no effect on it. Does it instead walk straight to a road that asked for one, and does a donkey in a warehouse count the same as one from the start stock? (Answered: it walks out to the farm's flag and goes home to the warehouse with the lowest route cost that accepts donkeys, as the simulation does; it never goes straight to a road. Implemented, T11a.) | ECO-FOOD implementation | answered | `ECO-food-mining.md` ECO-FOOD-6.3 |

The simulation links the shipwright to the player's first unfinished ship whose field is within the working radius (8) of the shipyard (not exclusive: several shipwrights may work one ship), and starts a new ship on the first walkable Coast field in flood order within that radius that holds no ship. Which fields are ship sites (water, coast, distance to the shipyard, own territory, how many ships at once), is a ship linked to one shipwright, how long does "puts the plank down" take (the simulation: no time), where does the shipwright stand (the simulation: on the site field), what is the text of the "ship complete" message (the simulation: "Ship finished."), and what happens to the finished ship (launch, plan 4.7)? (Answered: an unfinished ship of the player with no shipwright linked, on the shipyard's continent, nearest within 8 (exclusive link, released at each step end); new site = first Coast field in a walkable flood within 8 with a Water, non-doodad, ship-free neighbour (last of order 4,5,0,1,2,3); the shipwright walks to the Coast field, puts the plank down with the usual put-down; 56 decrements per trip, no carry-over, 12 trips; message "Ship constructed"; launch is plan 4.7.) | ECO-FOOD implementation | answered | `ECO-food-mining.md` ECO-FOOD-7.3a, 7.3b, 7.4, 7.4a, 7.4b |
| Q-131 | A grain field's lifetime (ECO-OUT-6.5, ECO-FOOD-3.3): the simulation sets 3000 when the field is created, counts it down in every tick in which the field is not reserved and is not a sapling (the first decrement is in the tick sowing ends), and removes the field 19 ticks after the tick the count reaches 0 (counter 1 at the marking tick, 20 at the deletion, no step in the marking tick itself; a harvested field is gone 18 ticks after the harvest because the resource update follows the settler update). (Answered: 19 ticks (marked with counter 1, no step in that tick); the count pauses for any reservation, also the harvest-only reservation; it runs while growing.) | ECO-FOOD implementation | answered | `ECO-food-mining.md` ECO-FOOD-3.5 |

| Q-138 | ECO-DIST-1.6: which wares the re-check visits. The simulation visits wares with a storage destination that lie unclaimed at a flag or are carried to a flag (not those being handed into the warehouse), raises the counter first, then drops the destination at 200. Is a ware claimed by a carrier, or one in the last carry, re-checked as well, and is the counter raised before or after the limit test? | plan 3.6 (implementation) | answered 2026-10-24 (E-sim-007, investigation): every ware of the transport's list with a destination (orders included, not only storage trips) that lies at a flag and that no carrier holds or has claimed; the limit is tested first (counter below 200 and all tests pass: counter + 1), otherwise the counter is set to 0 and the next flag computed again; the destination is not dropped by this job but by the next storage run or matching when no next flag is found (ECO-REQ-5.7) | `ECO-distribution.md` ECO-DIST-1.6; `ECO-requests-dispatch.md` ECO-REQ-5.7 |
| Q-139 | What happens to what is on a road or flag the territory rules remove (TER-5.3, TER-6.3; same open points as Q-032 b, c)? The simulation, until answered: the carrier and donkey of a removed road walk home (TRN-CARRY-7.1, specified); a ware a carrier held is put down at the nearer end flag of the road; wares the carrier had claimed at a flag are free again; wares lying at a removed flag are deleted; a building whose flag is removed is removed with it (TER-6.2 would not remove it, its own field can stay inner land while the flag field is Border); no road is cut at a flag's removal other than the roads that end there. (Answered: road removal: carrier and donkey go home, a held ware is destroyed, a claimed ware stays at its flag, end flags stay; flag removal: its roads, then the building behind it with remains (put on the flag), then the flag with all wares on it, destroyed; requests those wares were meant for are open again; the same steps serve the player's commands, territory loss and conquest, which also removes the conquered building's old flag.) | plan 4.2 (implementation) | answered | `TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-6; `TER-territory.md` TER-5.3, 6.3; `MIL-conquest.md` MIL-CON-2.2 |
| Q-140 | MIL-PROM-3.3, MIL-SOL-3.1: (a) the evidence line for the enemy-distance setter says it "resets the accumulator"; the rules say only the first update does. Does every distance update (each 11 ticks) set the coin accumulator to 0? If yes, accumulators never carry over more than 11 ticks and T11 (three servings in a row) only holds within one matching run. The simulation resets on the first update only. (b) T11's third serving is a tie of 0.1 and 3 x (1/30): in 32-bit float arithmetic the sum is 0.10000001 and the second building would win; the simulation forms each sum in double precision and stores 32 bits, which makes it a tie (first in list order). Is the original's sum formed wider than 32 bits? (Answered: (a) only the first update resets; (b) no: logic arithmetic is rounded to float32 after every operation (x87 at single precision), so T11's third serving goes to the second building, 0.10000001 > 0.1.) | plan 4.4 (implementation) | answered | `MIL-coins-promotion.md` MIL-PROM-3.3, T11; `ECO-time-units.md` ECO-TIME-5 |

Does a soldier keep its health in the stock (so that releasing and re-requesting does not heal it)? Same for MIL-SOL-6.2: a headquarters soldier put back into the stock. (Answered: the simulation is right; the stock holds counts only, a soldier leaving it starts at its rank's maximum, medium-high.) | plan 4.4 (implementation) | answered | `MIL-attack-combat.md` MIL-ATK-7.2 |

If his home building is gone the return does not start (the job refuses); what he does then (a warehouse, TRN-FREE-6.4, as ours) and how a building that now wants fewer soldiers takes him were not read; 2026-11-16 day (E-mil-017, code reading): the homeless case answered: every tick a soldier outside checks his four links (home building, attack target, two fight links) and with none left is sent home as a settler without a building (TRN-FREE-6.4), so ours is right (MIL-ATK-4.4); open: how a home building that now wants fewer soldiers takes a returning one | `MIL-attack-combat.md` MIL-ATK-4.4 |

| Q-164 | Observation from the computer player (plan 5.2), TRN-CARRY-3.2, 3.6, 4.2: a lone computer player on MP_2P_Storm_Coast stops growing after 30,000 to 100,000 ticks, in most seeds, with its road network in a gridlock: every flag along the chains between the headquarters and the outlying buildings holds 6 wares, wares bound for the headquarters (wood, stone, water, fish, corn, bread) and wares bound outward (planks, stone for construction sites) meet on single-lane chains, and every carrier on every road of the chain is loaded and waiting at the next, full flag (the headquarters' own flag too, so the warehouse cannot send anything). TRN-CARRY-3.6 only swaps two carriers' roads and needs one of them idle or loaded the other way round; it does not apply here, nor do the last place rule (a ware without a destination, put down by a producer, still fills the 6th place; giving it the direction of its storage trip did not help). Result of 8 seeds for 60,000 ticks: 806 to 2,636 fields owned, mean about 1,800; two computer players on the same map for 200,000 ticks never met. | AI implementation (plan 5.2 part 5) | answered 2026-10-16: the original never waits in that situation. The entry check (TRN-CARRY-3.2) first looks for a ware at the far flag that is ready for the way back along the carrier's road and, if there is one, claims it and enters regardless of room; at the flag it puts its ware down and takes that one in the same 4 + 4 ticks (TRN-CARRY-3.7). Also: a ware counts at the far flag from the moment its carrier is cleared (TRN-CARRY-4.3), and waiting wares enter in order of a wait score (TRN-CARRY-4.4). No larger capacity, no faster carriers, no direct warehouse intake beyond 3.3. Test T19 deadlocks under the old rules and flows under the new ones. (d) Production animals: drawn at 1 unit per metre at the world frame of their `boneLink` bone, `anim0` once per production start on a pose of their own. Their scale and whether the clip loops? (e) Ship bobbing: the global rate of the phase is not given (the view uses 1, so p advances 0.25 per second); pitch is taken about the ship's own x axis and roll about its z axis after the heading; a ship counts as moving when its tick positions differ; the ShipFX wake is not drawn; `idle` and `move` play on the `model` skeleton once launched. | 3D view implementation (plan 6.4 part 3) | partly answered (plan 7.5): (a) defaults enterDistance 0.3 / leaveDistance 0 (GFX-ANIM-6.1), (b) dying starts at the hunter's arrival (GFX-ANIM-5.2), (c) Working per work request inside the building (GFX-ANIM-6.2), (e) phase rate 2 pi (GFX-ANIM-7.2); (e) axes answered 2026-11-13 (GFX-ANIM-7.2a, E-gfx-066, T22): the first value turns the ship about its length (model x, heel to starboard), the second about its beam (model z, bow down), the second's turn first, then the first's, then yaw = heading + 90 degrees; moving = a movement step in progress; (d) open; 2026-11-16 day (bookkeeping): (d) the loop is answered by GFX-ANIM-6.3 / GFX-OBJ-1.6a (E-gfx-031: one clip started once per animal, looping forever, k of N shown by productivity); only their scale stays open | `GFX-animations.md` GFX-ANIM-5, 6, 7 |

The program draws only the background colour (0.8, 0.9, 1.0) beyond the skirt. (b) The skirt's water uses the world texture outside its 0..1 range: the program treats it as explored with no depth term (clamping would take the never-explored edge fields and draw the whole skirt black). What does the original sample there (address mode, a border texel)? (c) The main menu draws `ui_back` at 4:3 (full height, centred) with a dark surround (0.11, 0.05, 0.04) on wider screens, by the player's choice, where UI-MENU-1.1 stretches it over the whole screen; the campaign menu does the same. | 3D view and menus (plan 7, part 3) | answered 2026-10-17 (relabelled 2026-11-08 b; (a), (b), (c) answered): (a) and (b) answered (2026-10-17): the sky plane is a 981-unit cloud cap 15 above the camera that only shows at zoom 0; the skirt samples the world texture with clamp addressing (black while the edge field is unexplored); the camera pitch is asin(0.3 / 0.6 / 0.8) = 17.5 / 36.9 / 53.1 deg and the fov is 50 deg horizontal (near 1, far 500); (c) answered (2026-10-17): the original stretches `ui_back` over the whole screen and centres the 1024 x 768 parchment unscaled, exactly UI-MENU-1.1; the 4:3 letterbox with a dark surround is our deviation | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-3, GFX-VIEW-6, `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-6.2, `UI-menus.md` |
| Q-190 | Play-test, placement on the terrain (GFX-VIEW-1.4, 6.1, GFX-OBJ-2.3, TRN-FREE-5), stand-ins: (a) a build-help billboard centred on the ground (6.1) has its lower half behind the terrain when depth-tested; 6.1 says "no depth write" but not whether the depth test is on. The program moves each billboard 1.6 units toward the camera along the line to it (same place on the screen). Is the icon depth-tested, and is its centre or its bottom at the field position + (0, 0, -1)? (b) After working at a tree the woodcutter's walk back starts from the field before the tree (ECO-OUT-3.8), so he is drawn jumping from the end of his fine approach to that field; does the original walk the lattice back first, or snap? (c) The `GROUND` mesh is drawn as the decal of GFX-OBJ-2.3 with its own (flat) geometry, not draped over the terrain; on a slope it stands off the ground on the downhill side. | Q-202 | The view (GFX-VIEW-1.5, GFX-ANIM-3.5, 3.8a; play-test 2 part 2), stand-ins: (a) GFX-VIEW-1.5 lists the cell triangles as even rows (x,y)(x+1,y+1)(x,y+1), (x,y)(x+1,y)(x+1,y+1) and odd rows (x,y)(x+1,y)(x,y+1), (x+1,y)(x+1,y+1)(x,y+1); with GFX-VIEW-1.1's half step on ODD rows (and MAP-GRID-1.2's neighbours) these are not lattice triangles (on an even row (x,y) and (x+1,y+1) are 1.5 steps apart), so the list seems to use the opposite row parity. Which lattice is picked against, the fine height grid the terrain is drawn with or the field grid, and is "the field" the nearest vertex itself (then a fine vertex between fields needs a rule)? The program tests the ray exactly against the drawn fine triangles, takes the hit triangle's nearest vertex and then the field nearest that vertex. (b) GFX-ANIM-3.5: the tool is drawn while "the clip marks the item as shown (the work clips)"; the flag of the animation table is not listed, so the program shows it while a WorkLoop clip is requested and the worker carries no ware. Which clips carry the mark? (c) 3.8a: the tree turns by +angle about the axis tree -> woodcutter (right-hand rule in the left-handed game frame); the side is medium in the spec. The program counts the fall's first logic tick as the first tick after its start (work began + 3.0 / 1.8 s). (d) GFX-OBJ-2.3 takes the decal's texture coordinates "from the position within the box", but each building's `GROUND` mesh is a nine-patch of uv islands on `house_decal.dds` (corners, edges and centre from different parts of the texture; the woodcutter's hut: 44 vertices, 18 triangles), which one box mapping cannot reproduce; the program clips the terrain's triangles to each `GROUND` triangle and gives every point that triangle's uv by its barycentric weights (same draped geometry, texture as the mesh lays it). How does the original map the uvs, and is the box per triangle or per mesh? | Play-test 2, part 2 | partly answered 2026-10-17: (d) answered (GFX-OBJ-2.3: the program's mesh-uv draping is the original's; use wrap sampling, no v flip); (a), (b), (c) open; 2026-11-16 day: (a) answered (E-gfx-074, GFX-VIEW-1.5): the pick runs over triangles joining the field points (each corner the terrain vertex at the field's own sample), not the drawn fine triangles; the spec's row parity was swapped (even rows (x,y)(x+1,y)(x,y+1) and (x+1,y)(x+1,y+1)(x,y+1)); the first hit wins; the field is the hit triangle's nearest corner (strict, first-then-second, else third); (b) answered by GFX-ANIM-3.5 (E-gfx-010: the tool shows whenever no ware is carried); (c) open | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-1.5, `GFX-animations.md` GFX-ANIM-3.5, 3.8a |

(e) UI-GAME-1.10: the depot window's smart cursor target (it has a destroy button only for storehouses) is taken as that destroy button. | Play-test 2, part 3 | partly answered 2026-10-17 (Run B) | `UI-ingame.md` UI-GAME-1.8, 1.10, 3.4, 4.1 to 4.4, `TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-7.4, 8, `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-2.6 **Run B 2026-10-17 (E-gfx-012): (a) answered: the military window is the wide 466 px window (content to x 457, buttons in a row right to left from 407), UI-GAME-4.3.** **2026-11-16 day (E-ui-040, code reading): (b) answered, yes: the interface sends every click, and the logic tests the clicked field with TRN-ROAD-7.4 before the search, so a failing field extends nothing and road building goes on (UI-GAME-3.4 step 2); (c) answered: the right click cancels while the route is not complete (TRN-ROAD-8.3); a complete route only exists from a flag placed on its end until the next tick builds it, and then the right click does nothing (step 5). Ours cancels on any right click: right but for that window.** |
| Q-204 | Save games continued (FMT-SAV-2, 3; play-test 2 part 4, Q-195 c). The program reads the 2023 save to its last byte (every T5 count matches) and continues it as a game; observations against the spec: (a) a building's `Order Container` (3.4b 11) holds `logic UniqueId` refs, not `Village Order` objects (the orders themselves are in `Order System`), in both samples; (b) a flag's `Package Container` (3.9) holds `logic UniqueId` refs (the rewritten autosave has 6 at one flag); (c) `VillageDepot`'s FIRST `Stock` ("held wares" in 3.4a) is empty for every headquarters and storehouse in both samples and the wares are in the SECOND; the program takes the second as the warehouse's stock. What are the two? The fall starts at 3.0 s of game time into the work (W + 30) and lasts 24 ticks (step rate / 10); the removed deposit then sinks in the deposit renderer: from the harvest (not the pickup), y = y0 - 0.05 x removal counter x the height of the lying model's world box (7 to 9.5 units for the shipped trees), linear and interpolated per frame, removed at harvest + 18 (GFX-ANIM-3.8a, 3.8c, 3.8e, T11, T11a). The +x fall direction for an unseen woodcutter is still a stand-in (Q-300).** |

(b) the decal is lit like the ground with the house light (GFX-OBJ-2.3 'lit'): which colour does the pixel shader give (texture x light, an extra factor, the fog term)? (c) is the decal mesh scaled beyond the GROUND mesh's own size (the building's decal transform, the 'decal box' of the older reading)? An original screenshot at zoom 1 of a placed woodcutter's site would settle the size. | Construction stages (implementation, 2026-10-17) | answered | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-2.3a, 2.6a, T3g to T3i **Run D 2026-10-18: (a) sampler 0 is trilinear (linear min / mag / mip, no anisotropy), wrap, 11 mips; (b) colour = (ambient + max(0, N . -L) x light x shadow) x texture, alpha = texture alpha, then fog; N is the GROUND mesh's normal (up), not the terrain's; (c) no scale: the building's matrix only (-30 degree turn). With the shipped data the rule gives a soft-edged patch (alpha linear over 0.68 to 0.85 units, T3g); `site-foundation-2.jpg` matches that patch turned with the house plus the door strip, so a hard edge in the program is a departure from the rule (check the border uvs / alpha blending against T3g) rather than a missing feature.** |

It first runs a fine walk back (lattice line, fine speed) from the stand sample to the point of the field where the walk to the tree stopped (the rough walker's last field), ordered in the tick taking the ware ends and first moving in the next tick (so (b): nothing moves in W + 63); in the tick it arrives the free walk to the flag is ordered from that field, which first moves one tick later. The walk-to-field reset to the current field then moves nothing, as he stands on that field's point. Remove the slide.** |
| Q-215 | Shadow bias and map against Bevy's shadow map (GFX-VIEW-2.5 c, d; `docs/shadows.md`). The viewer keeps Bevy's own directional shadow map: the factor (2.3), casters, receivers and the 2 x 2 bilinear filter match, but (a) the spec's bias -0.00325 is in light-space depth, whose world size is 0.00325 x the light frustum's depth range; the program keeps Bevy's 0.02 world units toward the light plus a normal offset of 1.8 map texels the spec does not have. What depth range does the original's light frustum span at zoom 2 (ortho fit: near and far of the light projection), so the bias can be given in world units? (b) Bevy fits two cascades (1 to 80 and 80 to 300 units, 20 % blended), each 4096 x 4096; the original one map. What size is the map at each `SHADOW QUALITY` step, and is the default fit orthographic or LiSPSM? | Run A (implementation), 2026-10-18 | answered 2026-10-18: (b) Run D; (a) no single world size exists: the original always uses the LiSPSM fit, whose perspective warp makes the constant -0.00325 in normalised warped light depth correspond to different world distances across the view (smallest near the camera); the receiver body is the view frustum clipped by the shadow clip level plus each point's ray toward the sun up to 1000 units, cut by the visible casters' combined box (E-gfx-022). Keep an empirical bias that shows neither acne nor visibly detached shadows at zoom 1 to 4 | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.5, `SET-settings.md` SET-4 **Run D 2026-10-18 (E-gfx-016): the original's map is 128 x 2^round(4 q) texels for SHADOW QUALITY q (off, 256 .. 2048; 2048 in the sample profile) with the LiSPSM fit always, and the ground decal does receive shadows (GFX-VIEW-2.5 b, d).** |
| Q-300 | Standing deposits whose removal starts (Run D, GFX-ANIM-3.8c; E-gfx-014): the deposit renderer's sink writes the model's height each frame, but a grown tree in its plain state (2, not shaking) also rewrites its own matrix every frame; which write wins (the order of the two frame passes, the deposit renderer's vs the tree's) decides whether a tree removed while standing (lifetime end, the 60-tick fallback timer of 3.5 when the woodcutter's visual never started the fall, e.g. off screen) sinks or vanishes in place. Is the woodcutter's visual (and so the fall) also ticked while he is off screen? | investigation Run D | answered 2026-10-18 (E-gfx-022): the deposit renderer runs every element's own frame update first and writes the sink afterwards, so a tree removed while standing sinks upright; the settler view objects (and the fall trigger) tick for every settler, on screen or not (GFX-ANIM-3.8c) | |

Measured on `PB_R_Castle01` (the Roman HQ; its `_L` belongs to the material `baked_naked_CompleteMap`, set 1 spans 0..1): on the floor's up-facing vertices the light map averages (91, 94, 104)/255, 40 % of them below 0.25. In full sun the weight is (0.18, 0.31, 0.30), so the floor's (223, 194, 195) becomes (198, 156, 158), darker and redder; `hq.jpg` shows a light lavender floor, brighter than the ground, and ours drawn without the term matches it. Please recheck the term in `house_shader.fx`: is lightMap scaled (for example x 2), sampled from another set or channel, the weight different (a scalar from luminance rather than per channel), or the term off in the technique the game uses? | Play test 10, Part 1 (implementation), 2026-10-18 | answered 2026-10-18 (investigation, E-gfx-021): the term is as specified and active in every technique; the light map is the material's `Self-Illumination` texture (`PB_R_Castle01_L.dds`), loaded raw, set 1, no scale, per-channel weight; keep it. If `hq.jpg` still looks lighter, the cause lies elsewhere (see GFX-VIEW-2.6 for the whole pipeline) | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-2.1, T7; `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.6 |
| Q-220 | Builders freezing through shared hammer poses (play test 10, F8). GFX-ANIM-2.4 and 3.8f (b) say builders of one model file share one hammer pose, a pose is dropped only when its last user leaves, and a builder who joins a run-out pose holds its last frame for his whole step. Each builder leaves the pose for only one tick in 56 (the Stand tick of 3.8f a). So two builders whose cycles are offset by anything but 0 keep the pose alive forever: after the first 5.3 s it is never restarted, and both show the frozen last frame for nearly every step from then on. Measured (`--log-builders`, `MP_2P_Storm_Coast` with two normal AIs after 6000 ticks, six builders, speed 1): 4747 builder-frames drawn frozen in a work step within 1800 frames, the pose's users 3 to 5, whole 55-tick steps frozen; one builder alone freezes only in A + 53 .. A + 54 (T19a). Jens reports the original's builders do not freeze like this. Does the original share the pose across sites (and across players), or per site / per settler? Is a run-out play-once pose restarted when a new user attaches? A check for Jens: two huts built at once, both builders watched for a minute. | Play test 10, Part 2 (implementation), 2026-10-18 | answered 2026-10-18 (E-gfx-023): neither: the pool is per model file (all sites, all players), but a play-once clip (every work loop, pickup, put down, hit, random idle) is never shared: the settler always gets a pose of its own, a pose without users restarted at time 0 or a new one; only the looping clips (Stand, Walk, swim) are shared and joined at their current time. A user leaves a pose when his own clip changes; a pose whose last user left stops and is reused by the next clip at time 0. So builders never freeze through others; 3.8f (b), 2.4 and T12, T19b corrected, T19c, T19d added | `GFX-animations.md` GFX-ANIM-2.4, 3.8f, T19b |

Width 2.0 is right for the strip; the general road mesh is Q-092 | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-6.2, 6.4, 8.5 |

| Q-229 | Waterway buoys, caustics, sun and moon (GFX-OBJ-6.1, 6.2, GFX-WATER-2.5, GFX-VIEW-6.3; inventory run 2026-10-18 part 3), stand-ins: (a) a waterway is drawn as buoys only (`street_buoy.GR2`, no strip, since no waterway strip texture exists, Q-198 c), one every 1.7 units along the polyline of its field points at the water level, the first 1.7 from the start flag, none on the end, each turned along its step, not bobbing, casting no shadow. Where does the row start (at the flag, half a step in?), does it follow the field polyline or a curve, are buoys turned, do they bob like the border buoys (TER-7.3), and is any strip drawn? (b) caustics: the texture coordinate is read literally, u = x / 16 x 0.04 + f / 16 and v = z x 0.04, both wrapping, so the strip's neighbouring frames lie side by side every 25 units; is the frame offset added like that, or is the coordinate wrapped within frame f's slot? The blend runs from frame f - 1 (weight 0) to f (weight 1) by fract(16 frac(0.2 t)). t is the render time the water's waves use (Q-226). The term is added to the ground's colour in the terrain shader before the fog of war, which gives the additive pass's pixels; it is on for all ground below y -0.1. (c) the pass needs shader quality level 2: the program has no such setting and always draws it. Which settings field is the shader quality level? (d) sun and moon: "a quad drawn at the light direction x 100 from the camera": the program takes the light direction as GFX-VIEW-2.3 gives it (the way the light travels, pointing down), so the sun lies below the camera and the terrain drawn after it hides it; is it the direction toward the light? The quad is 30 (moon 9) units wide, faces the camera, and is drawn as a transparent object pushed back 4.5 times at 4.5 times its size (same picture) so the scene covers it, emulating "drawn first, depth test always"; it is drawn before or after the cloud cap by distance, not in a fixed order. Is the size the side or half of it, and is the quad turned to the camera? | Inventory run 2026-10-18, part 3 | answered 2026-10-20; (b), (c), (d): caustics coordinates and weights (GFX-WATER-2.5), the shader detail setting, and the sun: its quad is placed along the light's travel direction ((0, 0, 1) turned by heading and |elevation|), below the horizon for a positive elevation, so the program's reading is right (GFX-VIEW-6.3). (a) answered 2026-10-20: a waterway is buoys only, one pair per step at the step's curve midpoint, 1.7 to each side, untouched copies of the model, no bobbing (GFX-OBJ-6.2b) | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-6.1, 6.2, `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-2.5, `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-6.3 |

| Q-237 | ECO-HQ-4.3a: the leveller "then walks there (fine walk, TRN-FREE-5)", and the measured steps are 55 bulldoze ticks plus 28 or 23, the time of 2 lattice steps (TRN-FREE-5.3, factor 0.8 or level). Where on the lattice does the leveller stand while he levels a neighbour (the neighbour's own sample is 4 lattice steps from the site's; a point half way would give 2), where does his first walk start (the site's field, its flag?), and is the walk's slope that of the ground before or after the step's height change? | Q-238 | AI-CON-7.3 on MP_2P_Storm_Coast (the game of E-trace-002, player 1): s2-reborn's first stone pit and woodcutter run (game counter k 47, 4.8 game seconds; period 53 with c = k + 1) takes the home cell (8, 3), finds 0 stone producers and stones in the cell (map rocks on explored fields) and files a stone pit order of weight 10.
