What does the original's run see in its first runs: which cell the run's cursor takes first, how the stones are counted (map objects, or the AI's own resource squares, AI-2.4b, which may still be empty), and whether a further condition holds the order back? Added 2026-10-19 for Q-328: in s2-reborn the needs construction's order at that pick **is** the sawmill (138, 49)'s plank need, as in the original (weight 10, made at 6.3 s); the stone pit that wins is not a needs order but AI-CON-7.3's cell-slot order (4.8 s), older and so first by age x weight. The pick, the needs scoring and the tie rule agree with the original here; what differs is that the original has no 7.3 stone pit order then. | implementation, AI-CON-5 T21 (Q-320) | answered 2026-10-20 (E-sim-002): the stone pit / woodcutter run does not take the home cell: it keeps a cursor over the 11 x 11 cell grid (ceil(170 / 16)), starting at (0, 0) and advanced before each run, and tests the cell at that grid position if one exists; the runs at k 47 .. 312 look at (1, 0) .. (6, 0), where the player has no cell, so no order; the home cell (8, 3) is first looked at in run 41 (k 2167). The stones are the surface stone records of the shared low-resolution resource square of the cell on the cell's landmass (the maps the respawn reads, filled from the start); no further condition. The run also skips while its own order is pending. AI-CON-7.3 rewritten, 7.1 / 7.2 (list index) and 1.1 (validity cursor) stated, T22 | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-7.3, T21 |
| Q-239 | AI-2.7 b says the road-stop search (AI-CON-8.1 a, strategies 3 to 5) reads its path out with the free walk's rule (TRN-FREE-3.2a), drawing for the start and at every step where the kept direction is not allowed. That rule descends through labels, so the draws depend on the search that made them: (a) is the road-stop search the free walk's labelled search (TRN-FREE-2: step costs, the flood from the target T, the walkable-only first try and the second try) with road fields blocking except R's; (b) in a flood from T the fields of a road attached to T are reached first next to T, so which field is "the first reached that carries R" and how do the labels go on through it (one label per field, or a second layer after the noted field); (c) is the path read from F down to T, with its first draw also for a path of one step? s2-reborn's search is a breadth-first search from F with a "passed R" state, which has no labels to read out; without these answers the draws cannot be placed. | implementation, AI-2.7 b | answered 2026-10-19 (E-sim-002): (a) yes, the free walk's labelled search with the computer player's cost function (both directions summed, doubled on harbour fields; road fields block unless on the hit road R or the goal; flags block; a 4 x distance bound; excluded positions) and one try only; (b) one label per field, the hit is only a note taken when the first road field with T as an end is evaluated, after which R's fields are enterable; (c) the flood runs from F and the path is read from T down to F, the first draw being T's direction, none for a one-step path. AI-2.7 b extended. The draws in E-trace-002 cannot be matched yet: Q-329 | `AI-overview.md` AI-2.7 b; `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-8.1 a; `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-3.2a |

(b) T36 gives the clock at trace tick 67 as 6.799996, 72 as 7.2999954 and 73 as 7.3999953, while 5.11b says the clock in trace tick T is the float32 sum of T terms of 0.1, "7.2999954 at T = 73"; the sum of 67, 72 and 73 terms is 6.699996, 7.1999955 and 7.2999954, so the three rows are one tick ahead (the rows for 7 and 10 agree with the sum, and T35, which s2-reborn passes, uses the sum). s2-reborn follows 5.11b. Please correct the rows or the rules. | implementation, ECO-FOOD-5.10, 5.11b | answered 2026-10-19: (a) T27 corrected to the home-centred search; (b) the rows were labelled by the counter: corrected, and 5.11b now says what the animals actually read: in trace tick T the sum of T - 1 terms (the tick's advance of the clock comes after the animals), the recorded column of trace tick T being the sum of T terms. With that phase, and the wait computed in single precision (5.11b), the generator matches through trace tick 330 (two players) and 424 (one player) instead of 226 (measured in the investigation's experiment clone on 5e575d3) | `ECO-food-mining.md` T27, T36 |
| Q-241 | The first differing row of the two-player pair is now trace tick 178: the computer player's first constructor walking out of the castle door, fine (556090, 207819) in the original, (556090, 207820) in s2-reborn, and one thousandth apart in y at every later tick of the step (207639 / 207640, 207459 / 207460). `docs/trace-format.md` converts a walking position as from + (to - from) x progress, x 1000, rounded toward zero, so the original's progress is a hair below ours. s2-reborn keeps the step progress as integer millionths (0.045 = 45 000 a tick, exact). How does the original hold the progress and the per-tick increment (float32? the increment computed as base speed x slope factor / distance in float32 or double?), and is the completion test (TRN-MOVE-2.2, progress > 1.0) taken on that value, so that a float sum can complete a step one tick earlier or later than the exact one? | implementation, fidelity first differing tick 178 | answered 2026-10-19 (E-sim-002; the animals' mover is the settlers' interpolator): the step progress is a float32 to which the rate is added once per tick in float32; the rate is a float32 set at the step's start as 1 / (distance / speed) (speed the float32 base speed times the slope factor for settlers, TRN-MOVE-1.3; distance the lattice distance, 1); the step completes in the tick in which the progress exceeds 1.0 strictly (exactly 1.0 continues), the excess (progress - 1.0, float32) starts the next step; a walk starts at progress 0. The fine position follows from that float32 progress, so an integer millionths progress reads one thousandth high after the first tick of a step (0.045f = 0.0450000018). TRN-MOVE-2.1 to 2.3 should say float32; medium for the slope factor's arithmetic (not read) | `TRN-settler-movement.md` TRN-MOVE-1.1, 2.1, 2.2; `docs/trace-format.md` |

| Q-245 | AI-2.7 b as answered (Q-239): the road-stop search stops as soon as T is labelled, and the hit is the first field of a road ending at T that it evaluates. When F lies so that the flood labels T before it evaluates any field of such a road (in s2-reborn's test: T (24, 18), R from T to P (24, 24), F (27, 20) to the north-east), there is no hit. Is the path then still read out from T with its draws? The recording says no: with the read-out done in that case s2-reborn's generator parts from E-trace-002's at trace tick 156 (the hunter flag's attempt 3 at counter 155, the castle flag without a road, so no hit can exist), and without it the generator matches through 330 again; s2-reborn now reads out only with a hit. Does the strategy then simply fail (no R, no P; s2-reborn returns no stop), or does the original fall back to something else? Also: is a field of R counted as evaluated when it is tested as a neighbour but rejected for another reason (out of reach, not the player's), which decides whether such a field can be the hit? s2-reborn notes the hit only for a field that passed the reach, excluded, ownership, walkable and flag tests. | implementation, AI-2.7 b | answered 2026-10-19 (E-sim-002): confirmed, the read-out (and its draws) runs only when the search found T (state 2) and a hit is noted; without a hit the attempt yields nothing. The hit is noted inside the cost function when a road field q is evaluated as a step target with the road-stop mode on and no hit yet, after q passed the 4 x distance bound, the excluded positions and the player's usability test and before the flag, road and walkability (cost) tests; so a neighbour rejected by those three earlier tests is never the hit, one rejected by a later test is, one rejected only because it is a road field (not the hit road's) is the hit if its road ends at T | `AI-overview.md` AI-2.7 b |

| Q-253 | AI-CON-8.1d built (in a local build, not committed): our street construction now takes the hunter's flag (141, 48) as F at counter 134 and builds the first road at 176 from it to the castle's flag (140, 50), as T24 says; but our road planner (TRN-ROAD-2.3, A* from the start flag, field distance as estimate) returns "141,48 141,49 141,50 140,50", where the original's road is "141,48 140,48 139,49 140,50". Both cost the same in s2-reborn (when the search starts at the castle's flag our planner returns the original's fields, reversed). TRN-ROAD-2.4 leaves equal-cost ties open: does the original search from the target back to the start (so the stored list, from the start, follows the target's tie order), or how does its open list order equal f (first in first out, last in first out, by field index, by direction order)? Without this answer the cursor fix makes the first differing tick earlier (278 -> 224, the constructor's walk over the other road), so it is held back. | implementation, AI-CON-8.1d, TRN-ROAD-2.4 | answered 2026-10-20 (TRN-ROAD-2.3, 2.4, T17 .. T20): the search runs from the target, its open list is first in first out among equal label + distance, it stops the moment the start gets a label, and the road is read from the start: the neighbours with the lowest label below the current one, keeping the previous direction, else the lowest direction (0 before the first step). Gives "141,48 140,48 139,49 140,50" at 176. The rule also builds roads dearer than the cheapest (T18, T20, T6 now cost 34). Measured in a clone with AI-CON-8.1d: settlers 278 -> 254, buildings 566 -> 561, generator 366 -> 547 (matching 383 -> 568); 254 = TRN-CARRY-1.3's standing tick at the arrival flag, missing when it is the road's last flag; 561 = the leveller works (139, 49) where the original works (138, 48) | `TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-2.3, 2.4; `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-8.1d, T24 |

| Q-263 | Object numbers left open by SIM-START-4.4 (implementation, 2026-10-23): (a) which buildings make a production record when the computer player adds them: the sawmill makes one (T6 90212) and the hunter's hut none; s2-reborn takes the buildings with an input (a woodcutter, a stone pit, a fisher: none?); (b) how a flag put on a road numbers the split: is the old road removed and two roads made (4.3 c and a: four or six numbers), or does it keep its id with one new road; and does the half that keeps the carrier renumber the donkey network; (c) does a waterway's boat carrier join the donkey networks of its ends (4.3 b); (d) when a produced ware takes its number (when the product exists or when it is put on the flag) and when a builder leaving a ship at a landing does. s2-reborn's stand-ins: `docs/stand-ins.md` "Q-263" | implementation, day run 2026-10-23 | answered 2026-10-24 (investigation, E-sim-007, E-trace-006): (a) every building whose type has a product makes one, except the hunter's hut (so woodcutter, stone pit, fisher yes; hunter's hut 90716 at 5388 none), followed by its input needs; (b) a split removes the old road and makes two new roads (first half from the old road's first flag, second half from its second flag to the new flag), each with its join renumberings, then the carrier goes to its half with that half's donkey renumbering; measured to the last number at 1308 .. 1310, 4334 .. 4336 and 5388 (SIM-START-4.4 (j), T11); (c) yes: a boat carrier is given its waterway by the same step as a land carrier and joins the donkey networks (code only, medium); (d) a produced ware is numbered in the first tick of its worker's pick-up after the work, before it is put on the flag (seven cases measured, T12); the builder at a landing: Q-345 | `SIM-game-start.md` SIM-START-4.4 (b), (e), (f'), (j), T11, T12 |

| Q-265 | A ware taken out (ECO-DIST-1.7 step 4) and the door's wait (implementation, 2026-10-24): step 4 sends it "exactly as when a warehouse sends a ware for an order (ECO-REQ-5.3 (b))", and 5.3 (b) and ECO-REQ-3.7 set the warehouse's 30-tick send cooldown for a ware sent; but T12 has a plank leave on each of five consecutive ticks with t mod 7 = 0, which the door test of ECO-DIST-1.7c (a) would stop if a ware taken out set that wait (one plank per 35 ticks at most). Does a ware taken out set the door's wait, and is it carried out by a helper made at the door (5.3c) rather than appearing at the flag? s2-reborn sets the wait for a settler taken out (1.7a) and not for a ware, and puts the ware straight onto the flag (stand-in, `crates/sim/src/distribution.rs` `send_out`) | implementation, night run 2026-10-24 | answered 2026-10-24 (ECO-DIST-1.7 step 4, 1.7d, T12 corrected): yes, a ware taken out sets the door's 30-tick wait, through the same helper sending as an order's ware; the ware is made in that helper's hands at the door and is carried out and put down at the flag (ECO-REQ-5.3c), it never appears at the flag directly; so one unit per 35 ticks at most | `ECO-distribution.md` ECO-DIST-1.7, 1.7a, 1.7c, T12; `ECO-requests-dispatch.md` ECO-REQ-3.7, 5.3 |

| Q-269 | The start field of the street construction's flag search (implementation, 2026-10-24; the first difference at trace tick 464): AI-CON-8.1 searches "the nearest own flag T" for a building flag F with AI-CON-8.4, which starts "from a start field" without saying which. s2-reborn starts at F's field. For the sawmill's flag F (139, 48) at counters 344 .. 386 that search reaches the hunter's flag (141, 48) first (depth 2 through (140, 48), direction 0), so strategy 4 builds F to the hunter's flag first (road id 1) and F to the castle's flag second; the castle files and serves the hunter-side road's carrier request first (417, carrier 90234), where the original built 90224 (to the castle's flag 90182) before 90228 (requests 90232 at position 0 and 90233 at position 1, objects table, counter 387) and served 90232 at 417. Started from the building's field (138, 49), north-west of F, the search reaches the castle's flag first (depth 2 through (139, 49)) and the hunter's at depth 3; with that start (an experiment, not committed) the first differing trace tick moves from 464 to 494. Which field does the search start from, the flag's or the building's? (If the flag's, which other rule puts the castle's flag first?) | implementation, night run 2026-10-24 | answered 2026-10-24 (investigation, AI-CON-8.4, T29): the search starts at the flag's own field, but each queued field's neighbours are tested in the order 5, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 (one step in direction 4, then one step in each of 0 .. 5). From (139, 48) the first neighbour is (139, 49), taken from the queue first, and its direction 5 neighbour is the castle's flag (140, 50): T = the castle's flag at depth 2. Starting at the building's field is not the rule; it only gave the same T here | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-8.1, 8.4, T24; `TRN-carriers-transport.md` TRN-CARRY-1.3 |

| Q-272 | A road walk that ended short (TRN-FREE-6.5 e; 6.3: "what it then does is not traced"): a worker, builder, leveller, soldier or specialist whose road walk found no route at a flag on the way, or a carrier walking to its road. s2-reborn's stand-in: it stands on that flag with the rest of its walk and searches again each tick (TRN-FREE-6.1 from that flag to the walk's destination), walking on at progress 0 from the tick after one is found; going home chooses again (6.4, as specified). Earlier: partly answered 2026-10-25 day (TRN-FREE-6.3, medium, code only; E-trn-010): no retry anywhere; the ordering job counts as done once the settler stands; a builder or leveller not standing on the site's flag, and a soldier not on its building's field, go home at once (6.4), and going home itself does not unlink them from the building or site; a worker counts as arrived and its work routine starts where it stands (a worker without building goes home). a carrier on its way to its road retries: its idle step orders the walk to the idle field again (a new search) after each walk that ended elsewhere (E-trn-012). Answered 2026-11-13 (TRN-FREE-6.3a, high, code only; E-trn-033): the soldier, builder or leveller stays linked until his object goes (taken into a warehouse's stock the tick after his walk home ends, or dead on the way); the building counts him meanwhile and orders no replacement, unless it releases him as a not-present soldier (MIL-SOL-4.4 step 2); the site files a new builder or leveller request in its first update after he is unlinked (T45, T46). Open: what each work routine does from a far flag | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.3, 6.3a, 6.5 e |

| Q-278 | AI-CON-3.5's cell choice for player 1's coal target of counter 4847 (Q-365): with AI-CON-1.1c, 2.5a and 3.2 built, s2-reborn makes the target at the same counter and the expansion system takes it at 5330 as recorded, but at (104, 72), not the heap dumps' (116, 48). It scores every resource entry (good, kind, stored landmass of the fields holding it) with amount > 2 on a conquered continent, as 3.5 says since 2026-10-25 evening (E-ai-016). The conquered set is [12]. V is 240 for every cell coordinate and no cell has a production, so the score is the entry's amount + 240. Three cells tie at 352 with a 112-coal entry: (6, 4) (square (27, 19)), (7, 5) (31, 20) and (1, 8) (7, 35). (6, 4) wins as the first in the grid order (cy outer), and the 5 x 5 scan gives (104, 72). The recorded cell (7, 3) has at best 91 (square (30, 13)), so no ordering of the per-entry rule picks it. The experiment build exp4 gets (116, 48), but with the older rule of 62dbdae: square totals over the kinds, a square taken only when it lies on the home landmass (its own test, not per entry), and squares of the 4 x 4 cell. Which entries or squares does 3.5 score, which landmass test decides (per entry or per square, and on which field), and is the amount per square or per entry? The visible game is the same to 6000 either way: the guard house order of 5592 takes (128, 64) and its site (131, 59) at 5898 for both targets. | implementation, night run 2026-10-26 (Q-365 built) | answered 2026-10-26 night (AI-CON-3.5, 1.1c; E-eco-019): two readings were off. (1) The amounts: the cell-coordinate resource map holds per good, kind and stored landmass the sum over **all** the coordinate's fields; the heap dump of 5000 has coal on landmass 12: (7, 3) 252, (7, 5) 497, (1, 8) 182, (6, 4) 168, (7, 2) 98, (8, 3) 84, each equal to the map file's sum (7 per field), not 91 / 112 / 112 / 112. (2) V: S is not the best square in C but a square of the scored coordinate itself (its squares holding the good with no production, the one nearest to the square of C's position, C being the player's cell nearest to the coordinate), and P's distance term is the distance from that square to C's position's square: V falls with the distance, so (7, 3) at 252 can beat (7, 5) at 497. The last scan (5 x 5 squares, x outer) then gives (116, 48). Confidence high for both from code and the dump; the end-to-end choice is for the build to confirm | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-3.5, AI-CON-1.1b, 1.1c; `AI-military.md` AI-MIL-4.2, T16 |
| Q-279 | AI-CON-8.3 b, "B a depot": by the building's type (a harbour is a depot by type, AI-CON-1.1) or by the cell list it is filed in (harbours go to the harbour list)? E-trace-011, player 1's route task on the harbour 90351 (144, 47), cell (9, 2), finished at trace tick 6459: in the run of counter 6802 the original takes k = 2 (ai_route: cursor 90351, k 2, w 1, X [(145, 46)]), which is the depot branch's number of the castle's cell (8, 3), the castle being in that cell's depot list; at 6987 k = 3, then done, the cursor on to 90352 (7024) and 90436 (7061), whose run of 7172 calls the road builder (ai_plan (141, 52) -> (140, 54), no path), its stub taking number 90899. s2-reborn files the harbour in the harbour list and takes the branch "B finished and not a depot" (k = 0 the castle, then k = 1 the castle again), with the same w and X, stays on the harbour through 7172 and takes one number fewer from there: its object counter equals E-trace-011's through counter 7171 only. Open: (a) does "B a depot" hold for a harbour (and the headquarters, which is in the depot list), i.e. by type or by the depot and harbour lists; (b) in the depot branch, does a neighbouring cell's harbour count as "a depot" for S, and is "its first depot" taken from the depot list only **Answered 2026-10-27 night (investigation)**: by type. The record's depot flag is the building type's depot flag (headquarters, storehouses, harbours), whatever list the building is filed in; the depot branch reads only a neighbour cell's depot list (a neighbour's harbour does not count), S its first entry (AI-CON-1.2, 8.3 b, T45, E-ai-023). With it the experiment build's route task equals ai_route run for run to 7838; the next difference was the farm 90538 counted as a planting building (AI-CON-1.2 corrected: no product, T46) | implementation, 2026-10-26 (after MIL T18) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-8.3 b, AI-CON-1.1 |
| Q-280 | TRN-FREE-6.1 equal routes (T31): the read-out takes at each flag the first of its roads "in the order the roads were attached to it"; the spec says a split road's place in that order is not traced. s2-reborn orders a flag's roads by creation (road ids), and a road split by a new flag (TRN-ROAD-4.2) keeps its id for the first half (so that half keeps the old road's place at the old first flag and comes first at the new flag) while the second half takes a new id (last at the old second flag and second at the new flag): a stand-in. Open: where each half of a split road stands in the link lists of the old first flag, the old second flag and the new flag (E-trn-016: how the read-out reads the list is known; where the split inserts the halves is not read) **Answered 2026-10-27 night (investigation, code only, medium)**: neither half keeps the old road's place. The old road's entry is taken out of both end flags' lists (the others keep their order); then the half from the old road's first flag to the new flag is made and appended last at that flag and first at the new flag, then the half from the old road's last flag to the new flag, appended last there and second at the new flag (TRN-FREE-6.1, E-trn-016) | implementation, 2026-10-26 (TRN-FREE T31 built) | answered | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.1; `TRN-roads-flags.md` TRN-ROAD-4.2 |
| Q-281 | AI-CON-5.4, the site search's route ("a free-walk path, no road", medium): which free walk and how its length is counted. E-trace-011, player 1's military order of point (128, 32), radius 12 (limit 24), taken at counter 14394: the candidates (137, 38) (value 65) and (137, 37) (value 70.5) are both 12 fields from the point; the original places guard house 91373 on (137, 38) at counter 14449. A breadth-first route over walkable fields gives both length 12 (scores 32.5 and 35.25: (137, 37) wins, the buildings first differ at 14450). The free walk's first try (TRN-FREE-2.3 to 2.5, with its slope and DepositBlocked costs) read out without draws (as for the direct variant of AI-CON-8.3 c) gives (137, 37) 13 steps and (137, 38) 12 (35.25 -> 32.31 < 32.5): the recorded site, and the dense 20 000 pair's buildings first differ at 15181, the generator at 14764 (from 14469); s2-reborn uses that since 2026-10-26 (implementation). Open: (a) is the AI's route test this free walk (the first try only, or also the try with every field passable), (b) is its length the number of steps of the path read out, and with which choice at the start and where the kept direction is not allowed (no draw from the game's generator: the generator matches with it), or the cost label(W) scaled, (c) is it computed when the field is considered (s2-reborn) or once per search (Answered 2026-10-27 b: AI-CON-5.4b, T56. (a) the free walk's first try only, the search object of AI-CON-8.3 c's direct variant, flood from the candidate towards the start; no path: not a candidate. (b) len is a step count from the start down the labels, at every field the lowest direction number among the neighbours with the lowest lower label, no kept direction (unlike 8.3 c's read-out), no draws; the start 0, a neighbour 1 without search. (c) computed when the field is tested (after the cheap tests and only if its value is above the best score), every time. s2-reborn's kept-direction read-out gives the same count for all 349 site routes of the Weak 20 000 game; T56 (b) is a tie where it gives 6 and the rule 7) | implementation, 2026-10-26 (buildings point 14450 of the dense 20 000 pair) | answered | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-5.4, 5.4b (T56) |

(b) the matching releases the claim at once, always (its free-ware branch unlinks the ware's carrier link, both sides); (a) the next flag is not computed by the matching: the re-check computes it, in the same tick when it is a re-check tick (it runs after the matching), as at 14520; (c) the released carrier walks on, stands in his arrival tick and chooses work in the next (90399: stands on (137, 46) in trace tick 14579, walks back from 14580); T17 is the same rule (claim gone at 5764, next flag at 5766, 90399 claims again on arrival). | Q-284 | ECO-REQ-5.3 (b), 5.3c: a warehouse helper carrying a ware out while his warehouse and its flag are removed (here: lost in a fight, MP_4P_Fight soak, seed 1412607200, trace tick 23933, the helper of the storehouse (55, 45) at its flag field (56, 44), the order's site (58, 45) gone too). The specs do not say what happens to the ware and the helper. s2-reborn put the ware down on the field where no flag was any more (a soak invariant failure); the stand-in now, as Q-230 for a carrier walking into a building that is gone: the ware is gone (counted as used) and the helper finishes his round (pick-up, stand, back to the warehouse field, then home as any settler whose building is gone). Open: what the original does with the helper and his ware when the warehouse or its flag goes while he is out (and when the order's building goes: the ware is put down without a destination, ECO-REQ-5.5 stores it) (Answered 2026-10-27 b: ECO-REQ-5.3g, T22 (code, medium). The helper checks only his warehouse: at the door (no flag: home at once), at his arrival on the flag field (ware in hand and a building still on the warehouse's field: put-down as 5.3c, else home at once, no put-down or pick-up) and in every tick of the put-down and pick-up (building gone: home at once). Going home destroys a ware still in his hands (TRN-ROAD-6.3, 6.4: its order open again); it is never put down where no flag is. A flag removed while he walks only takes his ware off its list. When only the order's building goes he puts the ware down as usual, without a destination, and 5.5 stores it) | implementation, 2026-10-26 (soak) | answered | `ECO-requests-dispatch.md` ECO-REQ-5.3g (T22) |

| Q-287 | ECO-REQ-0.2, FMT-SAV-3.5: in which of the six settler lists is a settler that comes out of a warehouse's stock without a job of its own: one moved to another warehouse (ECO-DIST-1.7a), one leaving a burning warehouse (MIL-CON), one walking to a harbour for an expedition, and a donkey made by a donkey farm on its way to a warehouse (ECO-FOOD-6.2)? Is the class (SettlersWorker, SettlersCarrier, ...) chosen by the profession, and if so in which list is a helper (good_helper), which can be a worker (the well's helper is in the workers' list in E-trace-012) or a carrier? s2-reborn updates the six lists in the spec's order, each by object number (the original's lists hold ascending numbers in every tick of E-trace-002, 011 and 012), and puts such a settler in its profession's list as a stand-in: builder constructors, leveller levellers, helper, donkey and boat carrier carriers, the soldier ranks soldiers, geologist and scout specialists, every other profession workers (`World::list_of`) | implementation, 2026-10-28 night (ECO-REQ-0.2 in full) | answered 2026-11-11 eve (E-eco-044, ECO-REQ-0.2a): **the class (and so the list) is chosen at the warehouse door, by the request's kind when the settler is sent for a request** (a building's worker request makes a worker whatever the profession, so the well's helper is a worker as E-trace-012 shows; a road's carrier request makes a carrier, also for the donkey and the boat carrier; builder, leveller, specialist, soldier requests their own classes), **and by the profession when it is sent without one** (taken out by ECO-DIST-1.7a, or leaving a burning warehouse, where helpers are lost): builder -> constructors, leveller -> levellers, helper and donkey -> carriers, scout and geologist -> specialists, soldier ranks -> soldiers, every other profession -> workers. A donkey farm's donkey is a carrier. The program's stand-in (by profession only) differs for a helper hired as a building's worker: workers' list, not carriers'. Was: open | `ECO-requests-dispatch.md` ECO-REQ-0.2a |
| Q-288 | UI-GAME-1.10 (F50, play test 14): the original's double click that places a flag. Does a double click on a road (the road menu's "deploy flag") or a flag behave the same? s2-reborn, as a stand-in (UI-GAME-1.10c in our code): the second press of a double click (within the system's double-click time and rectangle of the first press, by the wall clock; Windows' defaults 500 ms, 4 x 4 px) that the dialog did not take, while the dialog the first click opened is open and the smart cursor is on, fires that dialog's first button (the smart cursor's target) once its panel is built | implementation, 2026-10-29 night (F50) | answered 2026-10-29 (code, E-ui-012): no double-click rule exists (no double-click window style, no system double-click time, no clock in the input layer); the "double click" is two ordinary clicks: the first opens the menu in the next frame update and the smart cursor moves the real pointer onto its first button, the second is a click on that button in any later frame update while the pointer is inside it (no time limit, no tolerance beyond the button); clicks are seen per frame, a press released before the next update is not seen by the map; the same for the road, flag and deploy-flag menus | `UI-ingame.md` UI-GAME-1.10c, T42 .. T45 |

Counting only the wares of served orders (the order travelling with the ware, ECO-REQ-5.3f) moves it to settlers 46700, buildings 47800, generator 46712 (implementation build of 2026-11-02 on b62baf5 with ECO-DIST T23, T24, ECO-FOOD T61). An order is a record the building adds to its own list, and the count takes those, open or served until delivered; a storage trip only sets the ware's destination (in the storage pass, no record). The difference to 46700 was the wares still in a warehouse helper's hands: counting the order-bound wares plus those helpers, without storage trips, gives Hard 48154 / 48034 (as by target), and no pair of the fidelity set moves (experiment build) | `SHIP-ships-expeditions.md` SHIP-5.2, E-ship-003 |

| Q-328 | The computer player's second order (AI-CON-5.2 pick): at the 50-tick of counter 294 the original takes the sawmill order (site (138, 49) at trace tick 324), s2-reborn the stone pit order (site (137, 51) at 324). Both orders exist from the start (stage 0 needs: stone and max(1, startSawmillCount) planks). Which order the pick prefers when both have age > 0: the sawmill (plank need) before the stone pit (stone need)? The notes give the sources' order 2, 1, 3, ... and the age x weight rule; the weights of the two needs and the tie rule decide. After that the third order (hunter (139, 47) at 597), the fourth (woodcutter (142, 56) at 819) and so on, as in Q-322's list. Pointer (E-sim-002): the needs construction run, when no order of its own is pending, scores every good that has a producer: (demand - supply) x the producer type's priority x (100 - stock term) x 0.01 / max(supply, 0.2) x a productivity factor (at least 0.1), demand = needs of the good with a cell, supply from the productions map built in the run (below); the good with the highest score (first on ties, in the goods map's order) gets the order for its first need with a cell and no production, weight 10; every need order has weight 10, so the pick at counter 294 only sees that one order. So the original's plank need outscored the stone need (or the stone need had no cell yet) at the run before 294; s2-reborn's stone need won. Check the two needs' cells, the stock of plank and stone then, the priorities and the run's tick. The three counts are lookups (map<good, int>, 0 when absent) in a map of the productions per good built in the run (supply), in a map reached through the object the goods map's node points to, and in the needs construction object's own map (its first field); which two quantities the last two are (stock in warehouses, goods ordered or in transit) is not read. | investigation, tick-for-tick run 2 | answered 2026-10-20 with Q-238: the original has no stone pit order at the pick of k 294 because AI-CON-7.3 walks the cell grid from (1, 0) and does not reach the home cell before k 2167; the pick, the needs scoring and the tie rule need no change (the implementation's note of 2026-10-19 on Q-238). The sawmill's plank need is the only order at 294. The count lookups of the scoring stay as read in the pointer (supply, a map of the goods map's node, the needs construction's own map); to be revisited only if the later picks of T21 differ | `AI-construction.md` AI-CON-5.2 |

| Q-354 | Road walk route choice (TRN-FREE-6.1, 6.2): constructor 90255 of E-trace-006 leaves the castle at counter 647 and reaches the castle's flag (140, 50) at 692; the road "140,50 141,50 141,49 141,48" was built at 660, between the two. The original's job state goes 0 -> 4 in the flag's arrival tick (691) and its first road step is east, (140, 50) -> (141, 50), over that road (towards the site 90244 at (139, 47)); an experiment build with the 660 road keeps the route it chose before (north-west over (139, 49)). And the same for going home (6.4)? | investigation, night run 2026-10-25 (Q-353's next difference) | answered 2026-10-25 night, second run (E-trn-009, TRN-FREE-6.5, T17..T19): a road walk can start only from a flag or a road field, so the first route is chosen when the road walk is ordered on the building's flag (the arrival tick; high, T17). The walk holds one road at a time: at every flag it reaches that is not the destination (also a flag set on its road meanwhile) the search runs again from there to the same destination and the walk goes on in the same tick. No route: the walk ends on that flag. The same for going home and a carrier's walk to its road, except "stop at the next flag" (going home started during a road walk, a carrier's road taken away). The new search at every flag is code only (medium) until a recording shows a route change at an intermediate flag | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.1, 6.5 |

Every other worker returns to its tool after carrying (woodcutter, stonecutter, hunter, ...). Which code drops the shipwright's tool good from the lookup (the tool object removed, or a flag of the item helper; E-gfx-010: the view uses the tool's good only while a flag of the tool object is clear), does it happen to every settler who takes a good out of its own building's stock and carries it outside, and does boat mode differ? | investigation, night run 2026-10-25 (settler clip check, TRC-29) | answered 2026-10-25 by the investigation (GFX-OBJ-7.4a rewritten, 7.5, GFX-ANIM-1.3; E-gfx-033): the tool is not removed; the settler's view shows it again only when a ware leaves his hand by being put somewhere. A ware destroyed while still in his hand (the shipwright's plank, used up at the ship) sends a "held ware gone" notice that sets the lookup good to none and leaves the tool hidden, until he next takes or puts down a ware; the shipwright never puts a plank down, so he never shows the hammer again. Every worker that puts its goods down gets its tool back. Boat mode is unaffected (no plank is carried). Measured: 559 hammer rows only before 39339, none of 11 041 rows from 39599 | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-7.4, 7.4a; `GFX-animations.md` GFX-ANIM-1.3 |

Q-361; there s2-reborn's route optimizer leaves building 90269's castle partner at counter 1067 by the direct variant (r = 3L = 30, the carrier of road 90277 holds a ware) while the original keeps the route variant (t = 2 at 1067), and later builds the road "141,48 141,47 141,46 140,46" that the original never builds). The costs go through a cache (per player and mode, keyed by the two flags' ids, either direction, -2 stored for no route), part of the checksummed state; the original used r = 10 at 1067, 1104, 1141 and 1178 though the carrier was busy and a road was built at 1163. The store is shared with the economy: the ware order matching (ECO-REQ-5.3) stores costs from the order's flag to the warehouses, which the computer player then reads (run 2140: stored 10 from 2002, a fresh 22 would have taken the direct variant). Run 1622: the walk path's length in the sum is its road length L of TRN-CARRY-5.2 (both directions, 20 here), not one direction (10), so 0 + 20 + 0 is not below r = 14. An experiment build with both follows the recorded route task run for run from counter 993 to 2584 (E-trace-010); fidelity's first ticks: settlers 1633 -> 1762, generator 1639 -> 1927, buildings 1838 -> 2962. Earlier: partly answered 2026-10-25 (AI-CON-8.3 c, E-ai-015; medium-high): the stored costs are dropped when the player's ware network changes (a road gets or loses its carrier or donkey); with that rule an experiment build follows the recorded route task run for run from counter 993 to 1585. Also corrected: the direct variant keeps only the last Fn in X (measured). Data (costs by road length L, which equal the original's heap values; fields = the walk's field count; the end the original takes): E-trace-014 counter 1649, carrier 90373 from the castle flag (140, 50) to road 90366 "140,46 140,47 141,48": 11 / 8, 5 / 3, the last flag (141, 48); E-trace-013 17631 (T39), road 92295 "139,58 140,58 140,59 141,60" from (141, 56): 8 / 8, 4 / 4, the first; E-trace-013 23131 (T41), road 93132 "137,60 136,60 135,59" from (140, 50): 29 / 28, 14 / 14, the first (also at every later flag); E-trace-018 48438, donkey 95715 from storehouse 94385's flag (134, 38) to road 91048 "142,43 143,44 143,45": 34 / 36, 16 / 15, the first (also at (135, 37), (137, 37)). The "fewer fields" rule of 6.1 (T41) gets the last case wrong (Weak game vs R5: settlers 48528), the cheaper-cost rule the third. The code (E-trn-027) sets the goal flag A = the flag the road references first, which the heap dumps show is the flag of field 0 for all four roads (90366: 90245 (140, 46); 91048: 91023; 92295: 91056), runs the flag search between A and the walker's flag (which end is the search's start, and so where equal routes are decided, is not settled from the listing), and ends the walk at the road's other flag B when the flag next to A on the read-out route is B (then the list's entry count - 2 compared with B). That fits all but the first case: from (140, 46) the route over (139, 48) costs 7 + 4 = 11 and the one over (141, 48) 4 + 8 = 12 (a unique cheapest route whichever end the search starts from), yet carrier 90373 went east (recorded: on (140, 50) at counter 1649 with a walk to (141, 50) at progress 0). What else differs for that walk (the search kind, stored costs, a different destination, the walk set up earlier)? | investigation, 2026-10-31 night | answered 2026-11-01: the walker's own road (here the destination road) costs 1 in its road walk search (E-trn-028: the search's own-road reference, passed from the walk's record). The goal is the road's first flag A; the search runs from the walker's flag, and the walk ends at the other flag B when the route comes into A over the road. 1649: to A over (139, 48) 11, over B 8 + 1 = 9, so B (east, as recorded); 48438: 34 against 36 + 1, so A; T39 8 against 9; T41 29 against 29, the older road into A wins. Experiment build: Weak settlers vs R5 48528 -> 49093, no pair earlier | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.1, T42 (T39, T41 corrected) |

Without an answer a click on the marked field would pass the step and open no window, and the next step's button would never show, so a click beside a ship selects it where it would open nothing else (`context_for`, stand-in). | implementation (2026-11-05) | answered 2026-11-05 (E-ship-004): (a) lesson 4's step 0x0a is **not a map click** and waits for no arrival: the ship window stays open from 0x08 to 0x0c, 0x09 expects compass button 2 (`!SouthWest`, sends go 2), 0x0a the window's **right fleet arrow**, which shows the player's other ship (the one `createShip` put at (127, 36), already in expedition mode next to the spot (129, 34)) and moves the camera there; 0x0b `!Construct harbor.` for that ship, 0x0c close (CAM-5.3a). The pointer field (127, 35) is never shown. The landing field search starts each ring after its start field (MAP-GRID-3): for (129, 34) it is (127, 34) (SHIP-6.1, 6.1a, T15). (b) A normal click finds a ship by its model under the pointer (a ray pick), after flag, building and road, and opens its window only when the field under the pointer has build class 0 (UI-GAME-9.4, T52). Open: whether the original accepts the harbour order of 0x0b (the created ship's last port position) | `SHIP-ships-expeditions.md` SHIP-6.1, `CAM-campaign-missions.md` CAM-5.3a, `UI-ingame.md` UI-GAME-9.4 |
| Q-1015 | `SND-sound-music.md` SND-8.3, SND-8.1 (stereo panning, built 2026-11-05 night): 8.3 says "Panning is the library's 3D panning from the listener's orientation (not specified further)", medium, and 8.1 that the listener's forward is D / \|D\| (with the slip of D = (v.y, v.y, v.z)) and its up vector was not read. So neither the right axis nor the pan law is known. The program pans by the place on screen: x = the sine of the angle between the camera's view and the sound along the screen's right, from the camera's eye; the speaker on the sound's side at full, the other at 1 - \|x\| (a balance), so a centre sound is as loud as before (`docs/stand-ins.md`). (a) What up vector does the game give the listener, and is the pan measured from the listener point L of 8.1? With forward D / \|D\| and up (0, 1, 0) a sound under the camera target would pan to the right by about 0.55 at zoom 1 (and about 0.45 measured from L along the screen's right), from the slip alone: is that what the original does? (b) FMOD 3.75's software 3D pan law: the pan value (0 .. 255) as a function of the direction, and the left and right gains for a pan (linear, equal power, or a balance)? (c) Does SND-2.6b's "level after distance and pan" take the louder side, the mean, or the pan's own level? | implementation (2026-11-05 night) | answered 2026-11-06 (E-snd-004; the code of `fmod.dll`): (a) up = Y - (f.Y) f, the pan from L of 8.1; (b) pan = 128 + trunc(128 n.R), R = up x forward, gains T(255 - pan) and T(pan) of v with T(p) = min(255, floor(16 sqrt p)): equal power, a centre sound at 180 / 181 of 255; (c) the mean (L + R) / 2 | `SND-sound-music.md` SND-8.1, 8.3a, 2.6b |

| Q-1018 | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-2.6, 4.3 (the mirror image; built 2026-11-06 night): the spec gives the technique (RenderWater with the mirror switch on), the texture's size and where it is read, and leaves open the mirror camera's matrix, the object kinds of the mirror pass and the clear colour. The program's stand-ins: (a) the eye mirrored in the water plane, drawn as an ordinary camera with its up vector mirrored too, so its picture is the reflection turned left to right and the water reads it at 1 - x; faces turned away from that eye are culled (so the land, seen from below, is not drawn; inverting the culling changed nothing in the measured regions); (b) the land and the map's objects (trees, stones, buildings), not settlers, animals, wares, roads or the sky cap; (c) cleared to the frame's background (the day table's, (0.8, 0.9, 1.0) at noon); (d) the reflection noise x (N_x, N_z) added to the projected position before the division by w. Measured on the look-compare pairs (`look-compare-auto/2026-11-06-night`, 2560 x 1440, region water = (100, 1150, 500, 200) of weakfogoff-*-cell63; original / ours without the mirror / ours with it): tick 1500 (47.7, 59.1, 46.7) / (47.2, 59.0, 42.4) / (49.7, 60.8, 43.9); 6000 (50.9, 60.8, 48.1) / (51.9, 62.0, 45.3) / (54.1, 63.5, 46.7); 11000 (14.2, 30.7, 31.0) / (14.9, 32.4, 29.6) / (14.9, 32.1, 28.9). The shallow film over the beach (600, 850, 300, 100) at 1500: (60.7, 53.6, 38.7) / (61.5, 59.2, 48.8) / (67.6, 63.5, 52.3). So the blue moves a third of the way and red and green overshoot by 2 to 3, and the film gets paler, not browner. With the surface's opacity there (0.2 x the opacity of 4.4), even a mirror image of pure (0, 0, 1) would add only about 1.5 of blue: the original's +4.3 needs more than the clear colour. What does the original's mirror texture hold over open water seen from the default camera (its clear colour, the sky cap or skydome, the land seen from below), which object kinds does the mirror pass draw, which culling does it use, and in which space is the noise offset added? | implementation (2026-11-06 night) | answered 2026-11-11 (E-gfx-049): every part is answered (the last, the depth byte, on 2026-11-09 below); with the original's interface left out the water agrees within 1.5 % by day down to -2, and the deep-water remainder is Q-387 (GFX-WATER-2.6c "Measured with the interface left out"). Before: partly answered 2026-11-06 (E-gfx-039): the camera (reflection then the main view, fov x 1.03, culling reversed, clip at the level), the content (sky alone when the view looks down more than 30 degrees: the default camera; else by MIRROR DETAILS), the background (the frame's clear with the cloud cap), the noise (before the division by w). Open: the rest of the +4 blue over open water (2.6c, T16a): a picture with the mirror switch off would separate it. 2026-11-07 (E-gfx-040): no further term in code (style values set per draw, unlit haze without channel swap, no post effect); the gap scales with the light and the surface weighs only about 0.07, so it lies in the ground under the water (sea-ground texture, wobble, haze); also corrected: ground below -7.05 is black (2.2, T9). Still open: the exact cause (2.6c, T16a). **2026-11-08 c (looks E-trace-021, E-trace-022 against E-trace-017, resampled to one camera): the mirror's share over open water at noon is +5.3 / +4.0 / +2.8 (warm, pale), none over the shore film; the original's pixel without the mirror is 42.3, 55.1, 44.1 in the water region. The program's mirror adds about half that and its pixel without the mirror is about 5 / 4 levels too red / green and 1.7 short of blue (GFX-WATER-2.6c table). Open only: how the remainder splits between ground, caustics and surface** **2026-11-09 (E-gfx-044, GFX-WATER-2.6c split, 4.2, 4.4 measured note, T16b, T16c): the look pictures are paused, so all their levels are 0.353 of the frame; the surface normal is the plain, unnormalised mean of the two wave texels (code); a per-pixel model of the region fits the measured mirror share and the ground (2.2, 2.3, 2.5 from the heights) only with a surface alpha of about 0.27 over open water, three times 4.4's rule (depth multiplier about 1, not 0.2), and with the cloud cap (0.70, 0.83, 0.92) as the mirror image. The program's half share follows from its 0.2 multiplier and its normalised normal (alpha 0.13). Open only: the depth byte's actual rule (a live read of the levels)**. **Answered 2026-11-09 (E-gfx-045; live read E-trace-025): the two words are set when the program starts by the map-geometry constructor (a static initializer): level -100, reference -3000 (read live: -100, -3000). So the surface lies at -0.1, not 0 (3.3), and the depth byte is trunc(255 x (1 - clamp((-100 - h) / 2900, 0, 1))): 255 (factor 0.2) on land and at the waterline, falling to 0 at -3000 (factor 1 from about -2420 on). Per surface point this gives the measured regions factors 0.84 .. 0.94 (alpha 0.24 .. 0.26), the cloud cap as the needed mirror image and the ground within 1 .. 9 %. 3.2, 3.3, 3.5, 3.6, 4.4 rewritten, T11 .. T15, T18** | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-2.6a, 2.6b, 2.6c, 4.3 |

Was: partly answered 2026-11-08 b (E-gfx-041; GFX-VIEW-5.4 f): the rectangle is copied every frame from the terrain view's visible field range (first / last column and row of the terrain cell renderer's view); its computation not found; **answered 2026-11-08 c (E-gfx-042, live read E-trace-021): the min / max over the drawn terrain cells (4 x 4 fields, ranges inclusive, neighbours sharing borders, last column / row ending at width - 1 / height - 1); the settler test is inclusive; the rectangle never leaves the map** (GFX-VIEW-5.4 f) | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-5.4 |
| Q-1032 | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.4 step 4 (2026-11-08 b: a homeless settler wanders between tries). Not specified: (a) the wait between two tries (the step alone, or a timer as well, and how long); (b) which neighbour the step goes to (the generator, a fixed turn, away from or toward something; the program takes direction tries mod 6, else the next walkable one round); (c) whether the count is the common part's third counter of a save (the 396 of the 2023 Coastal Storm save have 15 to 22) and whether it restarts when a later search starts a walk home (the program restarts it, and a loaded settler starts at 0); (d) whether "up to 51 times" means 51 failures in all (the program) or 51 repeats after the first. | implementation (2026-11-08 b) | answered (closed 2026-11-08 c). | Q-1034 | `TRN-free-walk.md` TRN-FREE-6.4 step 4 says a homeless soldier whose failure count passes 50 "looks for a military building (`MIL-soldiers.md`)" and, failing that, starts over with count 0. `MIL-soldiers.md` has no rule for that search. Needed: (a) which military buildings qualify (own player only; finished only; one that is missing soldiers, has room, or any; its "move soldiers out" or coins settings; a watchtower or catapult); (b) how one is chosen among several (road route cost from a flag, field distance, same landmass, a distance limit like step 3's 1000 or 30, list order on ties); (c) how the soldier gets there (road walk, free walk to that building's flag or to its network's nearest flag, as in steps 2 and 3) and whether he then enters it as a garrison soldier (MIL-SOL-2, counts towards its soldiers, its requests drop) or goes on looking; (d) whether the search draws from the generator or counts as a wander step for the count. So (a) to (c) do not arise: no building is chosen, walked to, joined or asked; (d) no generator draw, not a wander step. | Q-1035 | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.1 gives every terrain vertex "normal, tangent" and 2.4 lights the pattern's normal map in the frame they make, but no rule says (a) whether the vertex normal is 2.2's grid normal as a float or as its stored signed bytes trunc(n x 127.5), and (b) how the vertex tangent (and bitangent) is made. The program takes the float grid normal and the tangent as game +x made perpendicular to the normal (Gram-Schmidt), the bitangent n x t. The farm's ground band at 6000 (GFX-VIEW-2.4 note, inv-2026-11-08b item 1: the original 0.76 to 0.88 of ours on the levelled site's west ring, heights equal) is on sloped ground, where a different tangent (for example the row's finite difference (2 sx, h(c+1) - h(c-1), 0), which has no z part) turns the normal map's light. Checked 2026-11-08 c: our normals at samples (536 .. 541, 228 .. 232) are 2.2's from the same heights (Q-1030), so the tangent is what is left on our side. | implementation (2026-11-08 c) | answered 2026-11-09 (E-gfx-043): (a) the vertex normal is 2.2's stored bytes decoded (b / 127.5 - 1, not renormalised); the bytes are trunc((n + 1) x 127.5), unsigned (2.2 corrected); (b) the tangent is +x made perpendicular to that decoded normal, stored and decoded the same way; the shader makes B = T x N (2.1a). **The band's cause is not the tangent but when the terrain's copies are refreshed (2.1b):** after the first set-up they are refreshed only for samples with column and row up to the map's size in fields (0 .. 170 on Storm Coast), so the farm's ring is lit with the start's slope (T39: 0.65 .. 0.92 of the current normals' light, the band 0.76 .. 0.88) | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.1a, 2.1b, 2.2, 2.4, T38 .. T40 |
| Q-1036 | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-3.3, 4.1, 4.4 (measured note): 3.3 makes each water cell's quad its samples' bounding rectangle, so neighbouring quads overlap by the odd rows' half step (0.75 units in u: the next cell starts at this cell's last even-row column), and 4.1 draws them alpha-blended with no depth write, so the overlap strip is blended twice. With 4.4's rule (multiplier 0.2) that strip is faint; with the measured interim of 4.4's note (multiplier 1 over water deeper than about 1 unit, alpha about 0.27) it shows as a straight band of about 0.75 units at every cell column boundary over open water: built 2026-11-09 and drawn in the golden images (dialog-ship: a band about 10 px wide at x 326 .. 350, blue 20 .. 30 levels above the water beside it; start-paradise-z3 likewise; `target/golden-images` of impl-2026-11-09, run 00:00 PST). The original's open water in the look-compare pictures (weakfogoff-*-cell63, region water) shows no such band, and ours already had faint "angular light sheets" over the shore film (look-compare 2026-11-08, film region) that the original lacks. So 3.3 + 4.1 + 4.4's measured opacity cannot all hold. Which part differs in the original: (a) do neighbouring quads really overlap (the rectangle from the even rows only, or clipped to the next cell's start), (b) is the overlap blended once (a stencil, the draw order, a depth write after all), or (c) is the open-water opacity not 0.27 after all (then where does the measured mirror share come from)? The program keeps 4.4's rule (0.2 everywhere under water) and holds the interim back until this is answered (docs/stand-ins.md); the plain-mean normal of 4.2 is built. | implementation (2026-11-09) | answered 2026-11-09 (E-gfx-045): (a) the quads do **not** overlap. The cell's box (for culling) runs from its first field's position to its end field's and is widened by 3.0 units (half a field step) on the right to take in the odd field rows; the quad writer takes exactly those 3.0 units off the right edge again, so each quad runs from the first to the end field column's even-row u (24 units per cell inside the map) and the next cell's quad starts where it ends. 3.3's "bounding rectangle of the cell's samples" was wrong. (b) not needed: the pass has no stencil and no depth write, as 4.1 says; (c) the measured opacity stands: with abutting quads it draws no band. At the map's last column the right edge moves out 1.5 units per column cut off; the last row is not extended. 3.3 rewritten, constants, T17 | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-3.3, 4.1, 4.4, 2.6c |

The program keeps 4.6b step 2 for a new approach and continues a saved one as 3.5a (e) says: the saved walk from its point and progress, then the unit choice there, the unit's first walk starting from where he stands | implementation (2026-11-09 b) | answered 2026-11-09 c (inv-2026-11-09c item 3): (a) 4.6b step 2 holds: the field called the north-east neighbour is the flag field (the building's direction-1 neighbour, south-east with +y north, MAP-GRID-1.6), so every saved walk runs between the flag field's sample and the far point 3 direction-5 steps from it (that sample + (1, 3)); (b) the constructor's first saved number is the leg: 1 = on the way out (24602, 24675), 2 = on the way back (24454, 24416). | Q-1038 | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-4.5 against `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.5 (water shadows; our water receives none). 4.5 says shadows darken the surface to no less than 80 %, colour and alpha alike times clamp(shadow + 0.8), before the highlight. The implementation cannot build it from the specs as they stand: (a) GFX-VIEW-2.5 (b) lists the water among the surfaces that do **not** sample the shadow map. Which holds, and for which passes (RenderWater, RenderWaterNoMirror, the map border's passes of GFX-WATER-6.1)? (b) What is `shadow` in clamp(shadow + 0.8): the shadow map test's own result (0 wholly in a shadow .. 1 lit), so the floor is 0.8 at every hour, or the receivers' factor of 2.3 / 2.5 (1 lit, s in a shadow: 0.1 at noon, 0.8 at hour 0, 1 below 20 degrees of elevation), so 0.9 at noon and nothing at night? (c) Is the lookup 2.5 (c)'s (2 x 2 percentage-closer taps, bias -0.00325) at the surface point itself (height -0.1, 3.3), and is its light-space position made per vertex or per pixel? (d) "When on": is that the shadow quality setting above 0 (2.5 d), or another switch? (e) Order, as read: colour (4.3, mirror included) and alpha (4.4's opacity times the depth factor) both times the factor, then 4.5's highlight added, then 4.6's fog of war. Right? | implementation (2026-11-09 c) | answered 2026-11-09 c (inv-2026-11-09c item 2): (a) GFX-VIEW-2.5 (b) was wrong: the surface passes RenderWater / RenderWaterNoMirror test the map in the shadow techniques (Default / LowDefault), the map-edge skirt and the caustics never; (b) `shadow` is the test's 1 or the receivers' factor s of GFX-VIEW-2.3 (d), so the water's factor is 0.9 in a shadow by day and 1 whenever s >= 0.2 (no 80 % floor in practice); (c) one point-sampled tap per pixel from the per-vertex projective map coordinate at the surface (-0.1), its x, y moved by the mirror's wave offset reflectionNoise x (N_x, N_z) before the division by w, bias -0.00325, no 2 x 2 filter; (d) 'on' = the same switch as every receiver (shadow quality above 0, shader level 2); (e) yes: colour and alpha x factor, then the highlight (not shadowed), then fog | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-4.5a, T19; `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.5 (b) |
| Q-1039 | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-4.5a (b) (medium: "how far the offset moves the lookup on the map depends on the map matrix's w"). The water's shadow tap is moved by reflectionNoise x (N_x, N_z) (up to about 1.2 x 0.3) in the shadow map's projective x, y before the division by w. The implementation's map is orthographic (Bevy's cascades, w = 1; GFX-VIEW-2.5 d allows any directional map), where that offset would shift the lookup by about 1.2 x N_x x half the cascade's width, several fields, so it cannot take the offset as written. Needed: the shift the offset makes **on the ground, in world units**, for the original's LiSPSM matrix (2.5 d): e.g. for the default camera at noon (GFX-VIEW-3, 2.3), the world-space length of a projective offset of 0.1 at the screen's centre and at its top and bottom edge, or the matrix's x / y scale and the w over the visible water, so the implementation can move its tap by the same world distance. Until then the implementation takes the tap at the surface point itself (hard edges that do not wobble; `docs/shadows.md` difference 4) | implementation (2026-11-09 c) | answered 2026-11-11 (E-gfx-052, a live read of the shadow matrices at noon): on the water plane an x offset moves the lookup by one vector over the whole picture, (1.33, 1.37) world units per unit of offset at the default camera, and a y offset by k (c - p) toward a fixed point c, k = 0.0315 and c = eye + (63.0, 19.9) (centre (1.98, -0.96), top edge (1.98, -2.04), bottom (1.98, -0.24), left (3.21, -0.96), right (0.75, -0.96)); so the tap is taken at p + 1.2 N_x (1.33, 1.37) + 1.2 N_z 0.0315 (c - p), 0.2 to 1 unit for usual waves; the cell63 view at zoom 2 gives (1.77, 1.78), 0.0355, eye + (48.2, 11.4) | `GFX-water.md` GFX-WATER-4.5a (b); `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.5 (d) |
| Q-1040 | `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-3.5a (b) "State 33 of an indoor producer, step by step" (2026-11-10, low to medium) against `ECO-worker-cycle.md` ECO-WORK-3 (measured for workshops). The implementation can build the walk on (a loaded producer in 33 continues his free walk to his building's field from the saved step, k = 0), but not what follows it without guessing: (a) step (5), the two-part door animation: how many ticks each part lasts, and whether it is ECO-WORK-3.8's one-tick step inside or something else; (b) step (6), the work time "a table value scaled by the building's factor": is it the building's production time of ECO-WORK-3.2 (`bp_setProductionTime`, 333 ticks for the five sampled producers), and what is the factor; (c) the input: 33 has the building take it in step (2), before the walk, while ECO-WORK-3.2 has the building consume its inputs in the tick the work ends. After a load, does the building consume nothing more when the walker's (6) ends? (His input is already out of the saved stock.) (d) how 33's steps (1) .. (6) map onto ECO-WORK-3.1 .. 3.10: is (1)'s wait of 100 updates 3.9's rest? Does a workshop's worker walk (3) and (4) in every cycle? ECO-WORK-3 has no walk between the rest and the work. (e) after (6), does 36 begin with 3.3's take (4 + 4 ticks) and 3.4's wait for the flag? Until this is answered, ours keeps the stand-in: the two Coastal Storm walkers are seated inside at the load. | impl-2026-11-10-night (item 2) | answered 2026-11-11 (E-sav-014): **the 2026-11-10 item read the wrong state object and is withdrawn.** An indoor producer's 33 is only his production step: begun while the building is enabled (else 38), wait until it can produce, then one work action of the building's production time (code 4, no factor), and in the update it ends the inputs are consumed (ECO-PROD-3, ECO-WORK-3.2, ECO-PROD-4). So (a) there is no door animation in 33; (b) the work time is ECO-WORK-3.2's production time, unscaled; (c) nothing is taken before the work: a loaded producer working in 33 consumes his inputs from the saved stock when his saved ticks run out; (d) 33 = ECO-WORK-3.1 + 3.2 exactly, no wait of 100 and no walk; its saved state is one bool (1 waiting, 0 working; 70 of 70 samples fit); (e) yes, 36 is ECO-WORK-3.3 on. **The two Coastal Storm walkers are farmers, not producers** (100504, 298689): in the harvest step 8 (ECO-OUT-3.3) on the door step from their door field onto the farm's field, going out; next the flag, then the farm's reserved grain (ECO-OUT-3.4 .. 3.6), then 36 | `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-3.5a (b) 'State 33 of an indoor producer', 'A farmer going out to harvest' |
| Q-1041 | `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-4.1 (the classes and their versions, 2026-11-11) against `FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-1.6, 4.7 and FMT-SAV-2.1. Our save and map reader now expects every marker at the table's written version (the one difference, `MapInfo` 8 accepted, is removed: only 7 for maps and 9 for saves, as FMT-S2M-1.6 says). Three things don't fit together: (a) `MapInfo`: the table says its loader tests versions 1, 3, 4 and 6, but the release maps' version 7 has neither FMT-SAV-2.1's version-8 fields (`bool`, 8 player names) nor its version-9 `u32`, so the loader must also tell 7, 8 and 9 apart. Which comparisons does it make (would a version 8 be read with the names and without the `u32`)? (b) `Map Exploration`: the table says its loader reads one layout whatever the version (no "yes"), but FMT-S2M-4.7 (E-fmt-003) says it is read in two versions (0 stream `bool`s, 1 the state `u32`). Which holds? Ours reads 4 bytes a cell for both and takes the low byte in version 1. (c) The classes marked "yes" read older versions with fewer fields, but the table doesn't say which fields. Ours refuses every version except the written one, so a save of an older build is not read (no sample has one). Does any older build's save matter, and if so, which fields do those versions leave out? | implementation (impl-2026-11-11-eve, item 9) | answered 2026-11-12 (inv-2026-11-12 item 5, E-sav-016, code reading): (a) the `MapInfo` loader tests every version 1 .. 9 as 'at least' (FMT-SAV-4.1a): 8 is read with the names and without the `u32`, above 9 like 9; (b) FMT-S2M-4.7 holds: `Map Exploration` branches on its version (v0 cells stream `bool`s, v1 `u32` low byte, both 4 bytes), the table row was wrong; (c) the table's last column is a lower bound (stack-kept versions missed); older versions only come from older builds' files, which a reader of this build's files may refuse (FMT-SAV-4.1b) | `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-4.1, 2.1; `FMT-s2m-map.md` FMT-S2M-1.6, 4.7 |; FMT-SAV-4.1a, 4.1b
| Q-1042 | `UI-menus.md` UI-MENU-2.1 "The scroll bar" (2026-11-13) gives the bar's art and places; three things a player meets are not given, and ours stands in for them: (a) **the thumb's place along the track**: "slides along it with the scroll position". Ours puts its top at round(170 s / L) px from the bar's top, s the first row shown, L the last first row (maps - 11), so it is at the top with the list at its top and at the foot (194 - 24) at its end; with 11 maps or fewer it stays at the top. Is it in proportion, by which value (rows or pixels), rounded how, and is the thumb drawn at all when everything fits? (b) **what an arrow click does**: ours scrolls one row, held or not, and the arrows are never greyed (at an end a click does nothing); how far does a click scroll, does holding the button repeat, and is an arrow shown differently at an end? (c) **the track and the thumb**: does a click on the track page the list or move the thumb there, and can the thumb be dragged? Ours: neither (they take no clicks). The Save Game dialog's list (UI-MENU-5.1) is the same list box (E-ui-025) and still has our older arrows beside its first and last rows: is its bar at the same places (its rows are 22 px apart in ours, the plain style's 21 in 2.1)? | implementation (impl-2026-11-13-night, item 1a) | answered 2026-11-14 (E-ui-029, code reading): the bar holds a value v in 0..1 and the list follows it: first row s = floor(v (N - 11) + 0.5). (a) thumb top = 170 v px, not rounded; when everything fits the list sets v = 0.5, so the thumb is drawn **in the middle (85)**, s = 0. (b) an arrow click changes v by **1 / floor(N / 2)**, not one row (20 maps: 0.1, rows round(0.9 k)); holding repeats: a step at once, then whenever a 0.05 s countdown (lowered by each frame's duration) is used up; the up arrow is disabled at v = 0, the down at v = 1, both when everything fits (look when disabled not traced). (c) pressing the bar (track or thumb) moves v each update by 0.75 (p - v), p the pointer's height / 194: the thumb chases the pointer, so clicks and drags both work, no paging. The Save Game list is the same style (bar, arrows, 21 px rows at the same places). Ours should change: keep v as the state, the step, the middle thumb, disabled arrows, hold-repeat, the track press; Save Game's rows 21 px and the bar as in 2.1. Spec UI-MENU-2.1 'How the bar works', T18, T19 | `UI-menus.md` UI-MENU-2.1, 5.1 |
| Q-1043 | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-3.2 (3) (2026-11-13): each new settler figure gets its own block in the 1024 x 1024 atlas (16 x 16 blocks, so 4096 places), a block equal to one already there sharing its place. With 16 independent draws per figure nearly every figure makes a new block, so a long game (the view making figures again as settlers come back into it) can fill the atlas. (a) Are blocks ever given back (when a figure is removed, at a new or loaded game)? (b) What does the program do when the atlas is full: start again from the first place, stop making blocks and give the figure an existing one (which?), or something else? Ours keeps every block for the program run and, once the atlas is full, gives a new figure one of its colour map's blocks made before, picked from its drawn rows (the draws still made). (c) Does the atlas start empty at each new or loaded game (and the generator go on, as 3.2 (3) says it is seeded once per program run)? Ours: one atlas and one generator for the program run. | implementation (impl-2026-11-13-night, item 1a) | answered 2026-11-14 (E-gfx-067, code reading): (a) **yes**: each block has a use count (equal blocks share), a removed figure gives its block back and at 0 the place is freed; places are taken first-free in row order on the 64 x 64 grid, so a freed place is reused. (b) when no place is free the block gets none and the figure's draw looks it up without a check: undefined in the original (it needs more than 4096 different blocks in use at once); an implementation may pick any fallback, ours is fine for that case. (c) the atlas is made with each game's view, so it starts empty at every new or loaded game; the generator is not reset (seeded once per program run). Ours should change: free a block when its last figure goes (use count) and start the atlas empty at each game; keep one generator for the program run. Spec GFX-OBJ-3.2 (3a), T45 | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-3.2 |
| Q-1044 | `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-3.7a ("Checked against two saves with fights in progress", T17) against `MIL-attack-combat.md` MIL-ATK-6.1 ("a duel starts when both soldiers of a pair have arrived at the shared field"). In the two saves the soldiers' current fields (3.5h) of a fighting pair (list 2, both attack and defend objects at 27, 27) are not one field for two of the three pairs: attacker 16579 stands on (31, 21), the target building's own field, his defender 16378 on (32, 21), and the record's waiting field is (32, 21); attacker 16600 on (31, 19), defender 18545 on (32, 19), waiting field (32, 19); only 17868 and 18560 (and 17868 and the interceptor 16370) share (31, 19). (a) Where do the two soldiers of a duel stand: both on the waiting field, the attacker beside it (on which side, and why on the building's field in one case), or does 3.5h's field mean something else for a fighting soldier? (b) For an interceptor's duel the list-3 meeting field (32, 22) and the list-2 waiting field (31, 19) differ, and the pair stands on (31, 19): which field does the fight use, and what is the meeting field for once the pair has met? Ours restores both soldiers of a duel onto the record's waiting field (our duel needs both on one field). (c) The continuation (T17): ours, loaded from the first save, runs both duels on, but in 497 ticks the target falls (about tick +300) while the original still had two duels with two new defenders (18545, 18560); in ours the target's two new soldiers stay "not present" from about +60 to +300 and the two waiting attackers are never paired. Not a spec question by itself, noted for whoever compares: is there a rule that lets a soldier walking in to a building under attack be sent out to an attacker before he is inside? | implementation (impl-2026-11-13-night, item 1f) | answered 2026-11-14 (E-mil-010, code reading and the two saves): (a) **both stand on the record's waiting field, the attacker one lattice step off its point.** When the attacker begins the step in which he waits for his opponent there (also after being paired again), he alone takes one game draw, d = draw mod 6, and walks one lattice step from the field's sample in direction d (12 ticks on level ground); he counts as arrived only when that step ends. The defender stands on the sample and draws nothing. The stored field is the truncating field of the sample, hence (31, 21) / (31, 19) for two attackers (offsets (-1, +1)); nobody stands on the building's field. (b) The interceptor's duel is on the attacker's waiting field (31, 19); the meeting field is only where he went first (his first attacker's waiting field), and after a duel he waits where he stands. (c) No rule sends out a soldier still walking in: the defender choice takes idle soldiers (walk ended) or the target's interceptors in defense wait, with no other presence test; the original's 18545 and 18560 are reinforcements created after the first save (order 18544 open in it), who ended their walk and were then chosen. Lead for ours: find why its two new soldiers stay 'not present' 240 ticks (their walk in should end like any soldier's). Implementation: restore each soldier on his saved sample (not both on the field's point); add the attacker's step and its draw; meeting field only for the first walk. Spec MIL-ATK-6.1a (new), 5.1, T19, T20; FMT-SAV-3.7a | `FMT-savegame.md` FMT-SAV-3.7a; `MIL-attack-combat.md` MIL-ATK-6.1 |
| Q-1045 | `UI-game-setup.md` UI-SETUP-1.8 'What a click on them does' (T18, 2026-11-13 night) with UI-SETUP-1.6 (a) and 2.3. A new single-player free game makes slots 1 to 5 computers with their colour **unset** (-1, 1.6 (a)), and 1.8 draws the colour square from the slot's own colour c at (520 + 24 (c mod 2), 216 + 24 floor(c / 2)), which has no cell for -1; the click steps c -> (c + 1) mod 8. (a) What colour square does such a slot show before any click (its set-up entry's colour, i.e. its number as in 2.3, or something else)? (b) What does the first click on it give: (-1 + 1) mod 8 = 0 (the human's colour), or the shown colour + 1? (c) Which colour does the game give a computer player whose slot's colour was never set? Ours stands in: an unset colour shows and plays as the slot's number, and a click steps from the shown one (slot 1: 1 -> 2). | implementation (impl-2026-11-13-late, item 2a) | answered 2026-11-14 (E-ui-028, code reading): **the colour is never unset.** UI-SETUP-1.6 (a) was a misread of the call's arguments: a new single-player free game makes slot i (1 .. 5) a computer with start position i, **colour i** and team i (LAN / internet: open slots with colour i, team i, position unset). Only a kind change in the slot's list passes through -1, and the row's values are written over it at once. So (a) the square shows the slot's number's cell, (b) the first click gives (i + 1) mod 8 (slot 1: 1 -> 2), (c) the computer plays colour i unless clicked. Ours' stand-in gives the same results; the implementation should make the slots with these values instead of -1 and drop the fallback, and also give **team i** (not -1): equal teams are allies (MIL-SOL), so with team i a human who picks team i is allied with computer i. Spec UI-SETUP-1.6 (rewritten), T19 | `UI-game-setup.md` UI-SETUP-1.6, 1.8, 2.3 |
| Q-1046 | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-6.2c and 6.3 against the terrain mesh of `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-2.1 (F58, Jens's game 2026-11-14: dirt roads show bare ground around flags where two or three roads meet). The crossing is six flat triangles from the flag's sample point to six corners at 2.25 put on the terrain, but the terrain under it is the fine lattice (1.5 x 1.3 units), so between the corners the ground can rise above the triangles: on MP_2P_Storm_Coast 1594 of 1934 land fields (every third field) would have a crossing partly under the ground, by up to 1.76 units. With the street pass drawn as 6.3 says (alpha blending, no depth write) and an ordinary depth test, those parts are hidden, and at a flag with two or more roads the roads stop a segment short (6.2a d), so a hole shows. (a) Does the original's street pass test depth for the crossings (function, any depth bias or lift), or is the crossing kept in view another way? (b) Is the original's crossing also hidden in part on uneven ground? Ours stands in: each of the six triangles is laid on the ground in pieces as the roads are (6.2a e), keeping the flat triangle's footprint, texture coordinates and normals (the corners' mixed by place), so it is drawn whole; a triangle with a corner beside a waterway stays flat (T44). | implementation (impl-2026-11-14b, F58) | answered 2026-11-14 b (E-gfx-068, the effect files; GFX-OBJ-6.3 "Depth"): (a) the street pass, crossings included, tests depth (less-or-equal), writes no depth, alpha-blends, no alpha test, **no depth bias and no lift** (nothing in the effect, the vertex shader passes the position unchanged, no bias state set in the code); (b) so yes: the flat crossing is hidden wherever the ground rises above it between its corners, in the original too; draping it is not the original's rule (the implementation reverted it, a02ee50). Whether that explains F58's bare patches is item 1 of inv-2026-11-14b (the picture of the original at junction flags) | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-6.2c, 6.3 |

| Q-1048 | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-6.2c, 6.3 (depth: less-equal, no write, no bias, no lift) and 6.2d (a) against ours (F58, after the road lighting fix of F60). Ours builds the crossing exactly as 6.2c says (six flat triangles, corners on the fine lattice's planes, the centre at the flag's sample) and draws it with depth test, and on MP_2P_Storm_Coast after 45,000 ticks of a computer player (2560 x 1440, by day) whole crossing triangles are hidden where the fine ground rises above them (inside the start territory the flat hexagon sinks by median 0.020, up to 0.30 units), so at flags with two or three dirt roads bare ground shows between the roads' last segment and the flag; the original's pictures (E-trace-026) show such junctions continuous. Which rule differs? Candidates: (a) the terrain the crossing is tested against (the terrain quality setting drawing a coarser lattice, field level, at the profile's quality?); (b) the heights of the fine samples around a flag (changed when a flag or road is placed?); (c) the corners' heights (taken from a coarser plane, or the flag's own height?); (d) a depth state of the crossing draw that differs from the road strips'. Ours keeps 6.2c and 6.3 as written meanwhile. | implementation (impl-2026-11-14b, F58) | answered 2026-11-14 b (E-gfx-069, code reading; GFX-OBJ-6.2a d "When the count is taken", T47): none of (a) .. (d): (a) the terrain drawn and the corners both use the fine lattice (MAP-GRID-4.5, the corner search), the main depth buffer is 24 bit (D24S8); (b) flags and roads do not change the ground (E-trace-011: heights at ticks 0 and 20000 differ only around building sites, levelling); (c) the corners are on the fine terrain planes as 6.2c says, and the crossing builder makes seven flat vertices, draping nothing; (d) the crossing is drawn in the street pass. In the original, too, the flat crossing is mostly hidden on uneven ground (at the pictured flag (80, 112) of E-trace-026, 96 % of it lies up to 0.007 units under the ground). The rule that differs is **when a road decides to stop short**: with the count of roads at its flag when its strip is built, and a strip is not rebuilt when later roads join; so the first road at each flag runs to the flag point and covers the junction | `GFX-objects.md` GFX-OBJ-6.2c, 6.2d, 6.3 |

| Q-1060 | `SHIP-ships-expeditions.md` SHIP-7.1 and `CAM-campaign-missions.md` CAM-5.3 (step 0x0b): E-trace-030 saw lesson 4's second ship, made by the mission's script at (127, 36), show `!Construct harbor.` and found the harbour on the island (the click made a harbour site), so SHIP-7.1 accepts it there. SHIP-7.1 needs the ship's last port position; what is it for a ship a mission's script makes (`createShip`): its own field, the nearest harbour spot, or something else? Ours: such a ship has none, so the command is refused (the lesson still goes on, as CAM-5.3 says); its window shows the button anyway (UI-GAME-4.5 f, as seen), while a ship with a port shows it only where SHIP-7.1 holds. | implementation (2026-11-15 day) | answered 2026-11-16 night (E-ship-005, code reading): a ship `createShip` makes gets, at its creation, its **first land neighbour** as last port position: the six neighbours tested from direction 4 then 5, 0, 1, 2, 3, the first inside the map that is land of a land continent (ring 1 only; none if no neighbour qualifies). Lesson 4's second ship (127, 36) gets (128, 36); continent 36's spot (129, 34) is 3 fields away, so SHIP-7.1 holds, the button shows and the command is accepted. The same lookup sets the position at an expedition's arrival (was "nearest land field"); docking sets the **harbour's own field** (not the docking place); every route start clears it; a shipyard ship starts with its coast field. The window shows `!Construct harbor.` exactly while SHIP-7.1 holds, with no exception (SHIP-2.7, T16 to T18) | `SHIP-ships-expeditions.md` SHIP-2.5, 2.7, 6.1, 6.4, 7.1; `CAM-campaign-missions.md` CAM-1.2, 5.3a; `UI-ingame.md` UI-GAME-4.5 f |

| Q-1063 | `CAM-campaign-missions.md` CAM-3.3 target 3 ("Collect 100 gold coins"): E-trace-031 saw a free game with this target go on with 106 coins in the headquarters (from `GiveMeSomeMore`) for about 2 300 ticks. | Q-1066 | `UI-ingame.md` UI-GAME-8.5a (Q-198 d, E-ui-042) against `ECO-outdoor-workers.md` ECO-OUT-3.11: (a) series 6 averages the buildings that have a productivity entry, "made at its first finished work cycle"; ECO-OUT-3.11 (measured, T26) says the entry is made when the building's site is placed, at 0.5, for every type with a product (the forester's at his first planting). Which is it for the series: does a finished building that never completed a cycle (its entry 0.5 from the placement) count, and does a construction site with its entry? (b) series 0 counts owned fields "on a land continent" over the map's land fields: does a field of continent index -1 (mountains, other blocked ground, FMT-S2M-4.9) count as land, in the numerator and the denominator? Ours until answered: land in hundredths of all fields; goods with the workshops' inputs; productivity over the finished buildings with a worker, one that never produced as 0. | implementation (2026-11-16 day) | answered 2026-11-16 day b (E-ui-042, code reading; land count data): (a) the series sums round(entry x 100) over every productivity entry whose building still exists, with no finished or worker test: the entries are ECO-OUT-3.11's, made at 0.5 when the site is placed, so a construction site counts at 50 and a finished building that never produced counts at its current entry; a type without an entry never counts (the forester's from its first planting); UI-GAME-8.5a's "first finished work cycle" was wrong and is corrected. (b) a field of continent index -1 does not count; numerator = owned fields on non-water continents; denominator = the map's land-continent field count, the u32 after the continent objects (FMT-S2M-4.8; equal on 34 of 34 maps, e.g. MP_4P_Fight 2548) | `UI-ingame.md` UI-GAME-8.5a; `ECO-outdoor-workers.md` ECO-OUT-3.11 |

Still open: a sector's size in fields and the order in which the visibility pass lists the sectors (a spatial query not followed) | `GFX-world-view.md` GFX-VIEW-1.5 |
